    *   **Reliance on BC:** The performance of the agents are heavily reliant on Behavior Cloning, which is a weakness in a reinforcement learning setting.

**Justification for Score:**

The WEBAGENT-R1 paper makes a worthwhile contribution to the field of web agent training by demonstrating the effectiveness of an end-to-end multi-turn RL framework. The strong empirical results and the detailed analysis of training dynamics and ablation studies provide valuable insights. While the components are, on their own,  incremental, their combination into a working system and the specific application to web agents represent a significant engineering achievement. The WebAgent-R1 framework appears more effective and possibly easier to implement than existing solutions.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Beyond Static Testbeds: An Interaction-Centric Agent Simulation Platform for Dynamic Recommender Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16429v1)
- **Authors**: Song Jin, Juntian Zhang, Yuhan Liu, Xun Zhang, Yufei Zhang, Guojun Yin, Fei Jiang, Wei Lin, Rui Yan
- **Abstract**: Evaluating and iterating upon recommender systems is crucial, yet traditional A/B testing is resource-intensive, and offline methods struggle with dynamic user-platform interactions. While agent-based simulation is promising, existing platforms often lack a mechanism for user actions to dynamically reshape the environment. To bridge this gap, we introduce RecInter, a novel agent-based simulation platform for recommender systems featuring a robust interaction mechanism. In RecInter platform, simulated user actions (e.g., likes, reviews, purchases) dynamically update item attributes in real-time, and introduced Merchant Agents can reply, fostering a more realistic and evolving ecosystem. High-fidelity simulation is ensured through Multidimensional User Profiling module, Advanced Agent Architecture, and LLM fine-tuned on Chain-of-Thought (CoT) enriched interaction data. Our platform achieves significantly improved simulation credibility and successfully replicates emergent phenomena like Brand Loyalty and the Matthew Effect. Experiments demonstrate that this interaction mechanism is pivotal for simulating realistic system evolution, establishing our platform as a credible testbed for recommender systems research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Beyond Static Testbeds: An Interaction-Centric Agent Simulation Platform for Dynamic Recommender Systems":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RecInter, a novel agent-based simulation platform designed to address the limitations of traditional recommender system evaluation methods, specifically A/B testing (resource-intensive) and offline methods (inability to capture dynamic user-platform interactions). RecInter distinguishes itself by incorporating a robust interaction mechanism. User actions (likes, reviews, purchases) dynamically update item attributes in real-time, and Merchant Agents can respond to user reviews, fostering a more realistic and evolving ecosystem. The platform's high-fidelity simulation is achieved through Multidimensional User Profiling, an Advanced Agent Architecture, and LLM fine-tuning using Chain-of-Thought (CoT) enriched data. The authors demonstrate that RecInter achieves improved simulation credibility, successfully replicating emergent phenomena like Brand Loyalty and the Matthew Effect, highlighting the importance of the interaction mechanism.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addressing a significant gap:** The paper directly tackles a key weakness in existing recommender system simulation platforms: the lack of a dynamic interaction mechanism. This is a crucial aspect of real-world recommender systems that has been largely overlooked.
*   **Well-designed platform:** RecInter appears to be a well-engineered system with several key components (Multidimensional User Profiling, Advanced Agent Architecture, Behavior Simulation Training) that contribute to its overall realism.
*   **Strong experimental results:** The paper provides compelling experimental evidence demonstrating the improved simulation credibility of RecInter, successfully replicating phenomena like Brand Loyalty and the Matthew Effect. The ablation studies effectively highlight the importance of the various components of the platform.
*   **Leveraging LLMs effectively:** The paper demonstrates a thoughtful use of LLMs for user profiling and agent behavior simulation, fine-tuning models on carefully curated CoT data to improve realism.
*   **Reproducing interesting phenomena:** The platform's ability to reproduce emergent phenomena like Brand Loyalty and the Matthew Effect is a strong indicator of its potential to be a credible testbed for recommender system research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM Bias and Hallucination:**  The paper acknowledges the limitations of LLMs in generating subjective and inferred profiles. While efforts are made to mitigate hallucinations, the inherent biases in LLMs remain a concern. This could lead to skewed or inaccurate simulations. This concern is relatively high.
*   **Limited Action Space:**  The paper also acknowledges a predefined action space for agents. While this is understandable, it limits the simulation's ability to capture more novel or creative user behaviors.
*   **Interaction Model Scope:** The current interaction model focuses on user-item and merchant-item dynamics but doesn't explicitly model user-to-user social influence, community dynamics, or the impact of external events. This limits the platform's ability to simulate more complex recommendation ecosystems.
*   **Dataset Dependency:** The experiments are performed on a limited number of datasets. The generalizability of the results to other datasets and domains is not fully established. GoogleLocal is used frequently in experimentation, and this is quite limiting due to domain.
*   **Evaluation Rigor:** While the paper presents various evaluation metrics, it relies on certain LLM-based evaluations. The reliability and trustworthiness of these LLM-based metrics could be more rigorously verified. Additional human evaluations are performed, but could be more extensive.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The novelty of this paper primarily lies in the holistic integration of several key features, specifically the dynamic interaction mechanism (user actions affecting item attributes in real time), the merchant agents that can reply to users, combined with the use of CoT-enriched LLM agents. While individual components like LLM-based agents are not entirely novel, the combination and the focus on dynamic interaction provide a significant advancement over existing simulation platforms.

The significance stems from the potential to create a more realistic and reliable testbed for recommender systems research. This could enable researchers to explore complex system dynamics, test new algorithms under more realistic conditions, and better understand user behavior. The ability to reproduce emergent phenomena like Brand Loyalty and the Matthew Effect makes it a promising tool for studying real-world recommender system behavior. This addresses key weaknesses of the standard methods used.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field by addressing a crucial gap in recommender system simulation: the lack of dynamic interaction. The design of RecInter is well-thought-out, and the experimental results are compelling. The main weaknesses lie in the limitations of LLMs and the scope of the interaction model, which could be expanded in future work. Datasets are also a limited concern, where the conclusions may or may not hold in a wider variety. While not a groundbreaking theoretical breakthrough, the practical value of a more realistic simulation platform is considerable, justifying a score of 7.5. The ability to simulate real world phenomenon in the way presented is one of its strongest points, even with limitations in scope.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Implicit Jailbreak Attacks via Cross-Modal Information Concealment on Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16446v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaoxin Wang, Handing Wang, Cong Tian, Yaochu Jin
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) enable powerful cross-modal reasoning capabilities. However, the expanded input space introduces new attack surfaces. Previous jailbreak attacks often inject malicious instructions from text into less aligned modalities, such as vision. As MLLMs increasingly incorporate cross-modal consistency and alignment mechanisms, such explicit attacks become easier to detect and block. In this work, we propose a novel implicit jailbreak framework termed IJA that stealthily embeds malicious instructions into images via least significant bit steganography and couples them with seemingly benign, image-related textual prompts. To further enhance attack effectiveness across diverse MLLMs, we incorporate adversarial suffixes generated by a surrogate model and introduce a template optimization module that iteratively refines both the prompt and embedding based on model feedback. On commercial models like GPT-4o and Gemini-1.5 Pro, our method achieves attack success rates of over 90% using an average of only 3 queries.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework called "Implicit Jailbreak Attacks" (IJA) designed to circumvent safety alignment mechanisms in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). IJA conceals malicious instructions within images using least significant bit (LSB) steganography and couples them with benign, image-related text prompts. The framework incorporates adversarial suffixes (generated by a surrogate model) and a template optimization module to iteratively refine the prompt and the steganographic embedding based on feedback from the target MLLM. The experiments demonstrate that IJA achieves high attack success rates (ASR) on commercial models like GPT-4o and Gemini 1.5 Pro, requiring only a few queries on average.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a relatively novel approach to jailbreaking MLLMs by using implicit instructions hidden in images, thus avoiding reliance on explicit, easily detectable malicious textual prompts. The combination of steganography, adversarial suffixes, and iterative template optimization to adapt to different MLLMs also adds to the novelty. While steganography is a well-known technique, its application in conjunction with adversarial suffixes and template optimization for attacking MLLMs is innovative. A weakness, however, lies in the reliance on LSB steganography, which is a relatively simple technique that might be susceptible to detection with more advanced steganalysis methods.

*   **Significance:** The paper highlights the vulnerability of MLLMs to implicit attacks, even when they are aligned using safety mechanisms. The high ASR achieved by IJA on commercial MLLMs underlines the need for more robust cross-modal safety measures and raises concerns about the potential misuse of such models. This work contributes to a better understanding of the threat landscape for MLLMs and identifies a specific weakness that defense mechanisms should address. However, the evaluation is focused on a specific set of models and datasets, and the generalizability of the findings to other MLLMs and attack scenarios remains to be verified. Also, while the method achieves a high bypass rate, further analysis of the types of generated harmful content and the severity of safety violations would strengthen the significance of the work.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The IJA framework effectively conceals malicious instructions, bypassing safety filters.
    *   The adaptive template optimization mechanism improves attack performance across different MLLMs.
    *   The experiments demonstrate high ASR on commercial black-box models.
    *   The paper provides a clear explanation of the approach and its components.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on LSB steganography is a potential vulnerability.
    *   The evaluation is limited to a specific set of models and datasets.
    *   There is a lack of analysis of the characteristics of the generated harmful content.
    *   A better discussion on the limitations of the approach could be included, such as limitations in its reliance on textual prompt and its generalizability to other modalities.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper can inspire the development of more robust safety mechanisms for MLLMs.
    *   It raises awareness of the potential threats posed by implicit attacks.
    *   It can serve as a benchmark for evaluating the security of MLLMs against such attacks.
    *   It can guide future research on cross-modal alignment and safety in MLLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and technically sound approach to jailbreaking MLLMs. The results are convincing, and the potential implications are significant. However, the limitations related to the reliance on LSB steganography, evaluation scope, and analysis of the generated harmful content prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. Considering the novelty of the approach, its potential impact, and the existence of some limitations, the paper is assigned a score of 7.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Psychology-driven LLM Agents for Explainable Panic Prediction on Social Media during Sudden Disaster Events
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16455v1)
- **Authors**: Mengzhu Liu, Zhengqiu Zhu, Chuan Ai, Chen Gao, Xinghong Li, Lingnan He, Kaisheng Lai, Yingfeng Chen, Xin Lu, Yong Li, Quanjun Yin
- **Abstract**: During sudden disaster events, accurately predicting public panic sentiment on social media is crucial for proactive governance and crisis management. Current efforts on this problem face three main challenges: lack of finely annotated data hinders emotion prediction studies, unmodeled risk perception causes prediction inaccuracies, and insufficient interpretability of panic formation mechanisms. We address these issues by proposing a Psychology-driven generative Agent framework (PsychoAgent) for explainable panic prediction based on emotion arousal theory. Specifically, we first construct a fine-grained open panic emotion dataset (namely COPE) via human-large language models (LLMs) collaboration to mitigate semantic bias. Then, we develop a framework integrating cross-domain heterogeneous data grounded in psychological mechanisms to model risk perception and cognitive differences in emotion generation. To enhance interpretability, we design an LLM-based role-playing agent that simulates individual psychological chains through dedicatedly designed prompts. Experimental results on our annotated dataset show that PsychoAgent improves panic emotion prediction performance by 12.6% to 21.7% compared to baseline models. Furthermore, the explainability and generalization of our approach is validated. Crucially, this represents a paradigm shift from opaque "data-driven fitting" to transparent "role-based simulation with mechanistic interpretation" for panic emotion prediction during emergencies. Our implementation is publicly available at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/PsychoAgent-19DD.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PsychoAgent, a novel framework for predicting panic emotion on social media during sudden disaster events. It addresses challenges related to data annotation, risk perception modeling, and interpretability by integrating psychology-driven principles. The framework leverages a human-LLM collaborative approach to create a fine-grained panic emotion dataset (COPE). PsychoAgent integrates multi-domain data (physical event characteristics, risk communication, and individual traits) grounded in psychological mechanisms to model risk perception and cognitive differences. The framework enhances interpretability through a CoT-driven LLM agent that simulates individual psychological chains, which is verified by a MoE-based system. Experiments on the COPE dataset show PsychoAgent improves panic emotion prediction performance compared to baseline models, also demonstrating explainability and generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty of Approach:** The paper presents a paradigm shift from opaque, data-driven fitting to transparent, role-based simulation with mechanistic interpretation for panic emotion prediction. The combination of LLMs with psychological theories for a nuanced understanding of emotion is noteworthy.
*   **Dataset Contribution:** The COPE dataset is a valuable contribution, addressing the lack of finely-annotated data that previously hindered research in this area. The human-LLM collaboration ensures high quality and minimizes annotation biases.
*   **Model Design:** The proposed PsychoAgent framework is well-structured and incorporates relevant psychological theories about panic formation. The use of CoT and MoE systems enhances interpretability and ensures the generation of consistent, valid text.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The paper includes thorough experiments on COPE, demonstrating the effectiveness of the framework and validating its different components through ablation studies, scalability analysis, and case studies.
*   **Explainability:** A key strength is the emphasis on explainability. The model offers insights into the mechanisms that lead to panic emotion, moving beyond mere prediction accuracy.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM Hallucinations and Bias:** The paper acknowledges limitations related to LLM hallucinations and potential biases. While the MoE addresses some of this, it remains a concern. Moreover, the paper mentions that general LLMs may filter out negative emotions due to political correctness. How the research mitigates this issue for broader application could be more clearly outlined.
*   **Generalizability of the COPE Dataset:** The COPE dataset focuses on one specific disaster (Hurricane Sandy). While the approach can be generalized, the dataset itself might contain specific linguistic and topical biases related to that event, limiting its direct applicability to other disasters. A study exploring this limitation would be beneficial.
*   **Computational Cost:** While the scalability analysis is promising, the paper could have been more explicit about the computational resources required to train and deploy the PsychoAgent framework, specifically highlighting the cost of integrating and deploying several LLMs in its architecture.

**Significance:**

The paper is significant as it offers a novel and explainable approach to panic emotion prediction during disasters. The integration of psychological theories and LLMs opens avenues for developing more sophisticated and interpretable AI systems for emotion understanding and management. It paves the way for proactive governance, targeted interventions and improved emergency response strategies.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates solid novelty in its approach, introduces a valuable dataset, and showcases promising experimental results. The integration of psychological theories and LLMs for explainability is particularly strong. The limitations related to LLM bias and dataset generalizability slightly temper the impact. Therefore, a score of 8 reflects a paper that makes a significant contribution to the field with potential for future impact.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### MAGIC: Motion-Aware Generative Inference via Confidence-Guided LLM
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16456v1)
- **Authors**: Siwei Meng, Yawei Luo, Ping Liu
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in static 3D generation have intensified the demand for physically consistent dynamic 3D content. However, existing video generation models, including diffusion-based methods, often prioritize visual realism while neglecting physical plausibility, resulting in implausible object dynamics. Prior approaches for physics-aware dynamic generation typically rely on large-scale annotated datasets or extensive model fine-tuning, which imposes significant computational and data collection burdens and limits scalability across scenarios. To address these challenges, we present MAGIC, a training-free framework for single-image physical property inference and dynamic generation, integrating pretrained image-to-video diffusion models with iterative LLM-based reasoning. Our framework generates motion-rich videos from a static image and closes the visual-to-physical gap through a confidence-driven LLM feedback loop that adaptively steers the diffusion model toward physics-relevant motion. To translate visual dynamics into controllable physical behavior, we further introduce a differentiable MPM simulator operating directly on 3D Gaussians reconstructed from the single image, enabling physically grounded, simulation-ready outputs without any supervision or model tuning. Experiments show that MAGIC outperforms existing physics-aware generative methods in inference accuracy and achieves greater temporal coherence than state-of-the-art video diffusion models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MAGIC, a novel training-free framework for generating physically plausible dynamic 3D content from a single static image. MAGIC combines a pre-trained image-to-video diffusion model with an iterative LLM-based reasoning loop guided by confidence scores. This loop refines textual prompts and generates motion-rich videos tailored for accurate physical property inference.  The framework also incorporates a differentiable Material Point Method (MPM) simulator operating on 3D Gaussians reconstructed from the input image, allowing for physically grounded, simulation-ready outputs without supervision or model tuning. The authors demonstrate that MAGIC outperforms existing physics-aware generative methods in inference accuracy and achieves greater temporal coherence than state-of-the-art video diffusion models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel components and integrations. The core idea of using an iterative LLM-guided reasoning loop, driven by confidence scores, to refine video generation for physical property inference is a significant contribution. While pre-trained video diffusion models and LLMs have been used in other contexts, the specific way MAGIC combines them for this purpose is unique. Furthermore, integrating the LLM guidance with a differentiable MPM solver operating on 3D Gaussians is innovative.

*   **Significance:** The problem of generating physically plausible dynamic 3D content from limited input (like a single image) is important for various applications, including gaming, simulation, and robotics. Current methods often fall short in physical realism or require extensive training data. MAGIC addresses these limitations by offering a training-free approach that leverages the strengths of pre-trained models and iterative reasoning. If validated further, this could lead to more accessible and realistic dynamic 3D content generation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-free Approach:** The biggest advantage is the training-free nature of the framework. This eliminates the need for large, annotated datasets and expensive model fine-tuning, making it more scalable and applicable to diverse scenarios.
    *   **Iterative Refinement:** The confidence-guided LLM feedback loop enables adaptive learning and prompt refinement, leading to more accurate and robust physical property inference.
    *   **Integration with MPM:** Using a differentiable MPM solver operating on 3D Gaussians provides physically grounded dynamic simulation capabilities without explicit supervision.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes quantitative and qualitative evaluations, demonstrating superior performance compared to existing methods in terms of accuracy, robustness, and generalization.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** The framework's performance depends heavily on the quality and capabilities of the pre-trained image-to-video diffusion model and LLM. Any biases or limitations in these models could affect the generated content and inference accuracy.
    *   **Complexity:** The framework involves several components and a complex pipeline, which may make it challenging to implement and optimize.
    *   **Limited Scope:** While the paper demonstrates promising results, the experiments are limited to a specific set of scenarios and object types. It is unclear how well the framework would generalize to more complex or diverse environments.

*   **Potential Influence:** The proposed framework has the potential to influence the field of dynamic 3D content generation by:

    *   Providing a training-free alternative to supervised learning methods.
    *   Encouraging further research on LLM-guided reasoning for physical property inference.
    *   Inspiring new approaches for integrating generative models with physics simulators.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of dynamic 3D content generation. The training-free approach and iterative LLM-guided reasoning loop are particularly noteworthy. While the reliance on pre-trained models and the complexity of the framework are potential limitations, the experimental results demonstrate superior performance compared to existing methods. I am awarding the paper a score of 7 because it presents a strong and promising method, but further research is needed to address its limitations and validate its generality.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MMMR: Benchmarking Massive Multi-Modal Reasoning Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16459v1)
- **Authors**: Guiyao Tie, Xueyang Zhou, Tianhe Gu, Ruihang Zhang, Chaoran Hu, Sizhe Zhang, Mengqu Sun, Yan Zhang, Pan Zhou, Lichao Sun
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in Multi-Modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have enabled unified processing of language, vision, and structured inputs, opening the door to complex tasks such as logical deduction, spatial reasoning, and scientific analysis. Despite their promise, the reasoning capabilities of MLLMs, particularly those augmented with intermediate thinking traces (MLLMs-T), remain poorly understood and lack standardized evaluation benchmarks. Existing work focuses primarily on perception or final answer correctness, offering limited insight into how models reason or fail across modalities. To address this gap, we introduce the MMMR, a new benchmark designed to rigorously evaluate multi-modal reasoning with explicit thinking. The MMMR comprises 1) a high-difficulty dataset of 1,083 questions spanning six diverse reasoning types with symbolic depth and multi-hop demands and 2) a modular Reasoning Trace Evaluation Pipeline (RTEP) for assessing reasoning quality beyond accuracy through metrics like relevance, consistency, and structured error annotations. Empirical results show that MLLMs-T overall outperform non-thinking counterparts, but even top models like Claude-3.7-Sonnet and Gemini-2.5 Pro suffer from reasoning pathologies such as inconsistency and overthinking. This benchmark reveals persistent gaps between accuracy and reasoning quality and provides an actionable evaluation pipeline for future model development. Overall, the MMMR offers a scalable foundation for evaluating, comparing, and improving the next generation of multi-modal reasoning systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MMMR: Benchmarking Massive Multi-Modal Reasoning Tasks":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MMMR, a new benchmark designed to rigorously evaluate the multi-modal reasoning capabilities of large language models (MLLMs), particularly those employing intermediate thinking traces (MLLMs-T).  MMMR consists of a dataset of 1,083 challenging questions across six diverse reasoning types (logic, math, space-time, code, map, and science), combined with a modular Reasoning Trace Evaluation Pipeline (RTEP). The RTEP evaluates reasoning quality beyond just accuracy, considering relevance, consistency, and error types in the intermediate thinking traces.  The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art MLLMs, including both those with and without explicit thinking mechanisms, and find that while MLLMs-T outperform their counterparts, even the best models still exhibit reasoning pathologies like inconsistency and overthinking, falling short of human expert performance. The paper highlights persistent gaps between accuracy and reasoning quality, advocating for trace-aware evaluation for future model development.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers incremental novelty. While previous benchmarks have tackled multi-modal reasoning, MMMR distinguishes itself in two primary ways: (1) its explicit focus on *structured reasoning* depth (rather than breadth of task coverage) and (2) the emphasis on *evaluating the reasoning traces* themselves, not just final answers. While not revolutionary, these are useful improvements. The focus on MLLMs-T is timely given recent advances, and the modular RTEP for assessing reasoning quality is a valuable contribution. However, some existing benchmarks have started to incorporate some analysis of reasoning, albeit less extensively.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in several factors:
    *   **Targeted Evaluation:** MMMR provides a more targeted evaluation of reasoning capabilities, moving beyond solely assessing perceptual abilities or final answer correctness. This is crucial for understanding how MLLMs are actually solving problems.
    *   **RTEP Framework:** The RTEP offers a structured approach to analyzing the intermediate reasoning steps, allowing researchers to identify specific weaknesses in models' reasoning processes. The identification of reasoning pathologies is valuable for guiding future research.
    *   **Challenging Dataset:** The dataset is designed to be deliberately difficult, requiring deeper reasoning and cross-modal integration. This pushes models beyond their current limits, highlighting areas for improvement.
    *   **Actionable Insights:**  The paper identifies a disconnect between accuracy and reasoning quality, which provides actionable feedback for model developers.  It points to specific areas, like logical consistency and the reduction of overthinking, where improvements are needed.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Automation Reliance:** The RTEP framework relies heavily on GPT-4o (or similar LLM) for automated evaluation. While this enables scalability, it also introduces potential biases and inaccuracies from the evaluator model itself. The quality of the evaluation is thus tightly coupled to the evaluation LLM, which is acknowledged, but the impact of this coupling needs more thorough study.
    *   **Limited Error Analysis Depth:**  While error categories are identified, the analysis could delve deeper into the *causes* of these errors. More qualitative analysis and more detailed examples of the reasoning pathologies at play would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Dataset Scale:** While the dataset includes over 1000 samples, it is still a relatively small dataset for MLLM evaluation, especially considering the complexity of the tasks.
    *   **Generalizability:** The benchmark relies on a limited set of modalities (text, image, tables). Expanding to other modalities (e.g., audio, video) would improve generalizability.
    *   **Human in the Loop:** While the expert baselines are useful, a more comprehensive study on different human approaches to these reasoning problems would create a better comparison space.

*   **Potential Influence:** MMMR has the potential to become a standard benchmark for evaluating the reasoning abilities of MLLMs. The RTEP framework could be adopted by other researchers to evaluate reasoning in different domains. The paper's findings could influence the design of future MLLM architectures and training methods, encouraging a greater focus on reasoning quality rather than just accuracy. The insights into reasoning failures can help direct further research.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

I am assigning a score of 7 due to its strengths in addressing the gap in evaluating MLLM reasoning capabilities. The targeted dataset, the RTEP framework and associated insights are valuable for guiding future model development. However, the dependence on automated evaluation, the relatively small dataset size, and limited error analysis depth detract from the overall impact. While the paper contributes a useful benchmark, it is not a groundbreaking transformation of the field. The improvements over existing benchmarks are incremental and while useful, not revolutionary. A higher score would require a more innovative evaluation approach, deeper analysis of error causes, and a stronger demonstration of the benchmark's long-term influence.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### AnchorFormer: Differentiable Anchor Attention for Efficient Vision Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16463v1)
- **Authors**: Jiquan Shan, Junxiao Wang, Lifeng Zhao, Liang Cai, Hongyuan Zhang, Ioannis Liritzis
- **Abstract**: Recently, vision transformers (ViTs) have achieved excellent performance on vision tasks by measuring the global self-attention among the image patches. Given $n$ patches, they will have quadratic complexity such as $\mathcal{O}(n^2)$ and the time cost is high when splitting the input image with a small granularity. Meanwhile, the pivotal information is often randomly gathered in a few regions of an input image, some tokens may not be helpful for the downstream tasks. To handle this problem, we introduce an anchor-based efficient vision transformer (AnchorFormer), which employs the anchor tokens to learn the pivotal information and accelerate the inference. Firstly, by estimating the bipartite attention between the anchors and tokens, the complexity will be reduced from $\mathcal{O}(n^2)$ to $\mathcal{O}(mn)$, where $m$ is an anchor number and $m < n$. Notably, by representing the anchors with the neurons in a neural layer, we can differentiable learn these distributions and approximate global self-attention through the Markov process. Moreover, we extend the proposed model to three downstream tasks including classification, detection, and segmentation. Extensive experiments show the effectiveness of our AnchorFormer, e.g., achieving up to a 9.0% higher accuracy or 46.7% FLOPs reduction on ImageNet classification, 81.3% higher mAP on COCO detection under comparable FLOPs, as compared to the current baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AnchorFormer, a novel anchor-based efficient vision transformer designed to reduce the computational complexity of standard ViTs. The core idea is to use a set of anchor tokens to represent pivotal information within an image, enabling the estimation of bipartite attention between anchors and the remaining tokens. This reduces complexity from O(n²) to O(mn), where m << n. The anchors are learned differentiably using a neural layer, and a Markov process is employed to approximate global self-attention.  The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of AnchorFormer on image classification, object detection, and semantic segmentation tasks, showcasing improved accuracy and reduced FLOPs compared to existing ViT variants.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the integration of anchor tokens within the ViT architecture for efficient attention computation. While the concept of anchors has been explored in other areas (e.g., object detection), its application to the self-attention mechanism in ViTs, coupled with differentiable learning and a Markov process approximation, appears novel. The idea of learning the importance or "pivotal" regions through differentiable anchors is a good one. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental, as it builds upon existing efficient attention mechanisms like sparse attention and window attention. The novelty is primarily in the specific combination of techniques and the anchor learning process.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant bottleneck in ViTs: their high computational cost. By reducing the complexity of self-attention, AnchorFormer could make ViTs more practical for resource-constrained environments or high-resolution images. The demonstrated improvements in accuracy and efficiency across various tasks highlight the potential of this approach. The results presented are compelling and suggest a meaningful performance boost. If proven generalizable and scalable, it could have a noticeable impact on the deployment of ViTs in real-world applications. A potentially important application mentioned is ancient text analysis.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:**  The paper clearly articulates the challenges associated with the quadratic complexity of self-attention in ViTs.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** AnchorFormer is presented with a well-defined methodology, including the differentiable anchor learning process and Markov process approximation.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of AnchorFormer on multiple tasks and datasets, with comparisons against competitive baselines. Showing strong improvements across different tasks (classification, detection, segmentation) strengthens the credibility and generalizability.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components of AnchorFormer.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the paper provides an intuitive explanation for the anchor-based approach, it lacks a more rigorous theoretical analysis of its properties and limitations. For example, a more in-depth discussion about the error introduced by the Markov process approximation would improve the paper's rigor.
    *   **Anchor Number Sensitivity:** The ablation study on the number of anchors suggests a sensitivity to this hyperparameter.  More discussion on how to choose an appropriate anchor number for different datasets and tasks would enhance the practicality of the approach.
    *   **Potential for Overfitting to Pivotal Regions:** The focus on "pivotal" regions could lead to potential overfitting if the anchor learning process is not carefully regularized. Addressing the potential overfitting is critical.
    *   **Incremental Advance:**  As noted previously, the method builds on existing efficient attention techniques. The benefit of the specific combination of techniques used needs to be more explicitly highlighted.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future efficient ViT architectures. The core idea of learning a sparse, informative representation through anchor tokens is potentially valuable. The study may encourage researchers to explore similar anchor-based approaches for other transformer-based models.

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:** While the paper presents a novel and practically relevant approach to improving the efficiency of ViTs, its novelty is somewhat incremental. The significant empirical improvements are promising, but the limited theoretical analysis and hyperparameter sensitivity prevent it from receiving a higher score. More rigorous analysis and generalization studies could greatly increase its significance.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Reading Between the Prompts: How Stereotypes Shape LLM's Implicit Personalization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16467v1)
- **Authors**: Vera Neplenbroek, Arianna Bisazza, Raquel Fernández
- **Abstract**: Generative Large Language Models (LLMs) infer user's demographic information from subtle cues in the conversation -- a phenomenon called implicit personalization. Prior work has shown that such inferences can lead to lower quality responses for users assumed to be from minority groups, even when no demographic information is explicitly provided. In this work, we systematically explore how LLMs respond to stereotypical cues using controlled synthetic conversations, by analyzing the models' latent user representations through both model internals and generated answers to targeted user questions. Our findings reveal that LLMs do infer demographic attributes based on these stereotypical signals, which for a number of groups even persists when the user explicitly identifies with a different demographic group. Finally, we show that this form of stereotype-driven implicit personalization can be effectively mitigated by intervening on the model's internal representations using a trained linear probe to steer them toward the explicitly stated identity. Our results highlight the need for greater transparency and control in how LLMs represent user identity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates how Large Language Models (LLMs) implicitly personalize responses based on stereotypical cues present in user conversations, even without explicit demographic information. Through controlled synthetic conversations and analysis of latent user representations, the study reveals that LLMs infer demographic attributes based on stereotypes, which can persist even when users explicitly identify with a different demographic group.  The authors then explore a method for mitigating this stereotype-driven personalization by intervening on the model's internal representations, steering them towards the explicitly stated identity using a trained linear probe. The study highlights the need for transparency and control in how LLMs represent user identity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic exploration of stereotype-driven implicit personalization over the *course of multi-turn dialogues*. While prior work has shown LLMs are susceptible to stereotypes and engage in implicit personalization, this study takes a more in-depth look at how these inferences evolve during conversations.  The controlled dataset construction, which carefully isolates the effects of explicit demographic information and stereotypical content, strengthens the findings. The steering approach to mitigate undesirable effects of personalization is not entirely new (building on prior work on probing and steering), but its application in the context of countering stereotypes that overwrite explicitly stated identity adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a crucial issue concerning fairness and potential bias in LLMs.  The findings directly demonstrate that stereotypes can impact LLM behavior, influencing how the model represents users and potentially affecting the quality or suitability of responses they receive. This has important implications for real-world applications, particularly in areas like recommendation systems, story generation, or even sensitive domains such as hiring (as the paper notes.)

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The use of controlled synthetic conversations is a major strength, allowing for precise manipulation of variables and isolation of the effects of stereotypes. The study uses a combination of model-internal analysis (probing and surprisal) and behavioral analysis (examining model outputs), offering a more comprehensive understanding of the phenomenon.
    *   **Comprehensive scope:** The experiments span multiple demographic axes (age, gender, race, SES) and multiple LLMs, enhancing the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Mitigation strategy:** The investigation of a steering technique to mitigate stereotype-driven personalization is a valuable contribution, offering a potential pathway towards fairer and more controllable LLM behavior.
    *   **Clear presentation:**  The paper is well-written, with clear explanations of the methodology, results, and implications.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic data:** Despite the advantages of controlled synthetic data, it's crucial to acknowledge that the findings may not perfectly translate to real-world user interactions, which are far more complex and nuanced.
    *   **Limited mitigation success:** The mitigation strategy is more effective in steering towards explicit demographic identities than completely negating stereotypes when the user hasn't provided identity information. This suggests more work is needed in that area.
    *   **U.S.-centric stereotypes:** The stereotypes used in the data are acknowledged as largely U.S.-centric, limiting the generalizability of the findings to other cultural contexts.
    *   **Evaluation focuses on group membership:** The evaluation primarily focuses on whether the model correctly identifies or mentions demographic groups. A more nuanced evaluation could consider how these implicit inferences affect the *substance* and *quality* of the LLM's responses, going beyond simply detecting demographic mentions.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to stimulate further research in several directions:

    *   Developing more effective methods for mitigating stereotype-driven personalization in LLMs.
    *   Exploring the ethical implications of implicit personalization and the need for transparency and user control.
    *   Investigating the role of stereotypes in shaping LLM behavior in other domains, such as code generation or creative writing.
    *   Creating more diverse and representative datasets for training and evaluating LLMs to reduce bias.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by rigorously investigating stereotype-driven implicit personalization in LLMs across multi-turn dialogues. The use of controlled synthetic data, combined with model-internal and behavioral analysis, provides strong evidence of the phenomenon and the challenges involved. The mitigation strategy, while not a complete solution, offers a promising direction for future research. While the reliance on synthetic data and U.S.-centric stereotypes limits the generalizability, the findings are significant and likely to influence future work on fairness and bias in LLMs. The score reflects the paper's important contribution, despite certain limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Consistent World Models via Foresight Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16474v1)
- **Authors**: Yu Zhang, Xingzhuo Guo, Haoran Xu, Mingsheng Long
- **Abstract**: Diffusion and flow-based models have enabled significant progress in generation tasks across various modalities and have recently found applications in world modeling. However, unlike typical generation tasks that encourage sample diversity, world models entail different sources of uncertainty and require consistent samples aligned with the ground-truth trajectory, which is a limitation we empirically observe in diffusion models. We argue that a key bottleneck in learning consistent diffusion-based world models lies in the suboptimal predictive ability, which we attribute to the entanglement of condition understanding and target denoising within shared architectures and co-training schemes. To address this, we propose Foresight Diffusion (ForeDiff), a diffusion-based world modeling framework that enhances consistency by decoupling condition understanding from target denoising. ForeDiff incorporates a separate deterministic predictive stream to process conditioning inputs independently of the denoising stream, and further leverages a pretrained predictor to extract informative representations that guide generation. Extensive experiments on robot video prediction and scientific spatiotemporal forecasting show that ForeDiff improves both predictive accuracy and sample consistency over strong baselines, offering a promising direction for diffusion-based world models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Consistent World Models via Foresight Diffusion" addresses the challenge of achieving sample consistency in diffusion-based world models. Unlike typical generation tasks, world modeling requires outputs closely aligned with the ground-truth trajectory, and vanilla diffusion models often struggle with this due to the entanglement of condition understanding and target denoising. The authors propose Foresight Diffusion (ForeDiff), a framework that enhances consistency by decoupling condition understanding from target denoising. ForeDiff incorporates a separate deterministic predictive stream to process conditioning inputs independently of the denoising stream and leverages a pretrained predictor to extract informative representations that guide generation. Experiments on robot video prediction and scientific spatiotemporal forecasting demonstrate that ForeDiff improves both predictive accuracy and sample consistency over strong baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty of the paper lies in its architectural and training scheme designed to explicitly decouple condition understanding and target denoising in diffusion-based world models. The idea of using a separate deterministic predictive stream isn't entirely novel in the general machine learning landscape (auxiliary tasks are common), but its application within the specific context of diffusion-based world models, and the way it's integrated with a pretrained predictor, makes it a novel contribution. The analysis of the limitations of vanilla diffusion models for world modeling, particularly regarding consistency, also contributes to the novelty of the work.

**Significance:**

The significance of the paper stems from its ability to address a critical weakness of applying diffusion models to world modeling tasks. The paper identifies a key bottleneck, highlights the shortcomings of directly adapting diffusion models, and proposes a targeted solution. Successfully improving sample consistency has significant implications for downstream tasks such as planning and control, where reliable and predictable behavior is crucial. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of ForeDiff, making it a valuable contribution to the field.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the specific problem of sample inconsistency in diffusion-based world models.
*   **Insightful Analysis:** The analysis of why vanilla diffusion models struggle with consistency due to entangled condition understanding and denoising is insightful.
*   **Well-Designed Solution:** The ForeDiff framework provides a well-designed and theoretically sound solution to the identified problem.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experimental evaluation covers diverse tasks (robot video prediction and spatiotemporal forecasting) and uses appropriate metrics to assess performance.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of different components of ForeDiff (PredHead, number of ViT blocks).

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Improvements:** While the paper presents a novel approach, the performance gains, particularly in robot video prediction, could be considered incremental. The quantitative improvements in some metrics are not overwhelmingly significant, although the consistency gains are more pronounced.
*   **Limited Generalization Guarantees:** The reliance on pre-trained encoders may introduce dependence on dataset selection and reduce generalization. However, the benefits outweigh this limitation.
*   **Dependency on Auxiliary Predictor Pre-training**: The method depends on pretraining the predictor module. The design choice is motivated by the lack of existing well-suited pretrained models, but creates more work in training ForeDiff.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by identifying and addressing the critical issue of sample consistency in diffusion-based world models. The ForeDiff framework is a novel and well-designed solution that addresses this limitation through architectural decoupling and pre-training. The experimental results provide solid evidence of its effectiveness. The paper offers practical guidance for applying diffusion models in world modeling tasks, and its insights are likely to influence future research in this area.

Although the performance gains are incremental, these improvements address a key issue that previously restricted the performance of diffusion models in world modeling. Despite the above minor limitations, the proposed method successfully tackles a major problem in world modeling tasks using diffusion models and exhibits substantial improvements over the baseline diffusion models and other conventional models. Therefore, I believe a score of 8 reflects the novelty and significance of the work.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Advancing the Scientific Method with Large Language Models: From Hypothesis to Discovery
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16477v1)
- **Authors**: Yanbo Zhang, Sumeer A. Khan, Adnan Mahmud, Huck Yang, Alexander Lavin, Michael Levin, Jeremy Frey, Jared Dunnmon, James Evans, Alan Bundy, Saso Dzeroski, Jesper Tegner, Hector Zenil
- **Abstract**: With recent Nobel Prizes recognising AI contributions to science, Large Language Models (LLMs) are transforming scientific research by enhancing productivity and reshaping the scientific method. LLMs are now involved in experimental design, data analysis, and workflows, particularly in chemistry and biology. However, challenges such as hallucinations and reliability persist. In this contribution, we review how Large Language Models (LLMs) are redefining the scientific method and explore their potential applications across different stages of the scientific cycle, from hypothesis testing to discovery. We conclude that, for LLMs to serve as relevant and effective creative engines and productivity enhancers, their deep integration into all steps of the scientific process should be pursued in collaboration and alignment with human scientific goals, with clear evaluation metrics. The transition to AI-driven science raises ethical questions about creativity, oversight, and responsibility. With careful guidance, LLMs could evolve into creative engines, driving transformative breakthroughs across scientific disciplines responsibly and effectively. However, the scientific community must also decide how much it leaves to LLMs to drive science, even when associations with 'reasoning', mostly currently undeserved, are made in exchange for the potential to explore hypothesis and solution regions that might otherwise remain unexplored by human exploration alone.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) to transform scientific research, moving beyond simple tasks like literature review to more creative roles, including hypothesis generation and experimental design. It surveys current uses of LLMs as scientific "co-pilots," discusses the rise of foundation models trained on scientific data, and explores how LLMs might evolve into "creative engines" capable of fundamental scientific discoveries. The authors emphasize that for LLMs to be effective, they need to be deeply integrated into all steps of the scientific process, collaborate with human scientists, and have clear evaluation metrics. The paper also addresses ethical concerns related to creativity, oversight, and responsibility in AI-driven science and highlights the need for careful management of hallucination (generating inaccurate information), limited reasoning capabilities, and lack of transparency in LLMs. The authors advocate for a "LLM-in-the-loop" approach rather than a "human-in-the-loop" approach, emphasizing the assistance role of LLMs, and suggest a concept of "algorithmic confidence" to quantify the trustworthiness of LLM generated research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper provides a broad overview of the potential and challenges of LLMs in scientific research. It doesn't present a specific novel *technical* contribution, but rather offers a *perspectival* contribution. The novelty comes from its comprehensive framing of LLMs within the entire scientific method, from hypothesis generation to validation. While many papers focus on specific AI techniques for particular scientific problems, this paper attempts a holistic view. This holistic perspective is valuable, especially regarding the ethical and practical implications. Also, the suggestion of "algorithmic confidence" is potentially novel.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its broad appeal and timeliness. It synthesizes diverse applications of LLMs in science and discusses crucial issues like hallucination, reasoning limitations, interpretability, and ethical concerns. It serves as a valuable resource for researchers entering the field and for policymakers considering the impact of AI on scientific progress. The framework of considering LLMs across the entire scientific method helps to identify bottlenecks and areas for improvement. The exploration of the role of "hallucinations" as a possible, albeit controversial, source of creativity is also intriguing. The discussion about "algorithmic confidence" could potentially encourage more nuanced assessments of AI-driven findings.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** Covers a wide range of topics, from practical applications to ethical considerations.
    *   **Balanced Perspective:** Acknowledges both the potential and limitations of LLMs.
    *   **Timeliness:** Addresses a rapidly evolving field.
    *   **Clear and Accessible:** Written in a way that is understandable to a broad audience.
    *   The introduction of the term "algorithmic confidence" is a potentially significant contribution that merits further exploration.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Lack of Technical Depth:** The paper primarily surveys existing work and doesn't present new technical developments or rigorous experiments.
    *   **Generalities:** Some arguments are somewhat general and lack specific examples or concrete solutions.
    *   **Algorithmic Confidence:** The concept of "algorithmic confidence," while novel, could be developed more thoroughly. The paper does not provide precise methodologies or metrics for its implementation.
    *   **Limited Critical Analysis:** While the paper identifies many limitations, the analysis of existing research and their strengths could be more thorough and critical.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper provides a useful and timely synthesis of a rapidly evolving field. Its strength is the breadth and balanced perspective it offers. It serves as a valuable guide for researchers, policymakers, and anyone interested in the future of AI in science. While it lacks significant technical novelty and could benefit from deeper analysis, it makes a valuable contribution by framing the discussion, highlighting key challenges, and proposing new directions. The suggestion that "hallucinations" might be a source of potential creativity is a potentially innovative idea, as is the proposal for an "algorithmic confidence" metric.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Teaching Large Language Models to Maintain Contextual Faithfulness via Synthetic Tasks and Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16483v1)
- **Authors**: Shuzheng Si, Haozhe Zhao, Cheng Gao, Yuzhuo Bai, Zhitong Wang, Bofei Gao, Kangyang Luo, Wenhao Li, Yufei Huang, Gang Chen, Fanchao Qi, Minjia Zhang, Baobao Chang, Maosong Sun
- **Abstract**: Teaching large language models (LLMs) to be faithful in the provided context is crucial for building reliable information-seeking systems. Therefore, we propose a systematic framework, CANOE, to improve the faithfulness of LLMs in both short-form and long-form generation tasks without human annotations. Specifically, we first synthesize short-form question-answering (QA) data with four diverse tasks to construct high-quality and easily verifiable training data without human annotation. Also, we propose Dual-GRPO, a rule-based reinforcement learning method that includes three tailored rule-based rewards derived from synthesized short-form QA data, while simultaneously optimizing both short-form and long-form response generation. Notably, Dual-GRPO eliminates the need to manually label preference data to train reward models and avoids over-optimizing short-form generation when relying only on the synthesized short-form QA data. Experimental results show that CANOE greatly improves the faithfulness of LLMs across 11 different downstream tasks, even outperforming the most advanced LLMs, e.g., GPT-4o and OpenAI o1.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper, with a focus on rigorous justification:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CANOE, a framework to improve the faithfulness of Large Language Models (LLMs) in both short-form and long-form generation tasks. CANOE tackles the problem of LLMs generating contextually unfaithful responses, a significant challenge in building reliable information-seeking systems. It addresses this through two key components: 1) a method for synthesizing easily verifiable short-form Question-Answering (QA) data, using diverse tasks generated by GPT-4o, and 2) Dual-GRPO, a rule-based reinforcement learning (RL) approach that optimizes both short-form and long-form responses using tailored rewards derived from the synthesized QA data. The RL method avoids the need for human-labeled preference data and prevents over-optimization on the short-form task. Experimental results demonstrate CANOE's effectiveness across various downstream tasks, surpassing even advanced LLMs like GPT-4o in some cases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach by combining synthetic data generation with a tailored reinforcement learning framework (Dual-GRPO) to improve LLM faithfulness. While synthetic data generation and RL have been individually explored, CANOE's specific combination, particularly the design of Dual-GRPO and proxy reward construction, offers a new perspective on tackling the faithfulness problem. Furthermore, the use of various QA tasks contributes to data diversity. *However, the innovation lies more in the thoughtful orchestration of existing techniques than in fundamentally new algorithmic components.*

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a critical issue in the LLM field – the tendency to generate factually incorrect or contextually unfaithful outputs. Improving faithfulness is crucial for deploying LLMs in real-world applications where accuracy and reliability are paramount. The paper's claim of outperforming powerful models such as GPT-4o is impactful. *However, it's crucial to acknowledge the inherent limitations of evaluating LLM faithfulness, especially in long-form generation. While the use of MiniCheck is helpful, it provides an imperfect proxy. The results hinge on the quality of this evaluation metric.*

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The CANOE framework is well-motivated and clearly articulated.
    *   The use of synthetic data addresses the data scarcity problem and removes the need for human annotations.
    *   Dual-GRPO is designed to avoid pitfalls associated with focusing solely on short-form QA. The implicit evaluation of the long-form output through the proxy reward is clever.
    *   The experimental setup is comprehensive, including a range of downstream tasks and comparisons to strong baselines.
    *   Ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of individual components.
    * The paper provides a case study as well as a human evaluation, showcasing a qualitative improvement in model performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on GPT-4o for synthetic data generation raises concerns about potential biases and limitations in the generated data. While the paper introduces diverse tasks, the quality of the synthetic data ultimately depends on the capabilities of GPT-4o.
    *   The proxy reward, while innovative, isn't a perfect measure of long-form faithfulness. It is an indirect metric and relies on the ability of a subsequent prompting to drive the LLM to provide the right answer to a *second* short-form QA test. This makes it more complex to reason about.
    *   The generalizability of CANOE's approach to other LLM architectures and task domains needs further investigation. While the paper includes various datasets, the framework's robustness in highly specialized or low-resource settings remains unclear.
    * While the human evaluation provided helpful qualitative insights, a more detailed or broad human review may further support the validity of the study.
    * The number of participants in the human evaluation was not specified. A larger number of participants may lead to more concrete and reliable results.

*   **Potential Influence:** CANOE provides a valuable framework for improving the faithfulness of LLMs, particularly in scenarios where human annotation is expensive or impractical. The approach is potentially influential because it provides an accessible and scalable way to train LLMs to be more reliable and trustworthy. Future research could focus on refining the synthetic data generation process, developing more robust evaluation metrics for long-form faithfulness, and extending the framework to other LLM architectures and tasks.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper makes a significant contribution to addressing the crucial problem of faithfulness in LLMs. The use of synthetic data and the design of Dual-GRPO are innovative and well-motivated. However, the reliance on GPT-4o for data generation and the imperfect nature of the proxy reward limit the paper's overall impact and generalizability. Despite these limitations, CANOE offers a promising framework and motivates future research in this important area.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLaMAs Have Feelings Too: Unveiling Sentiment and Emotion Representations in LLaMA Models Through Probing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16491v1)
- **Authors**: Dario Di Palma, Alessandro De Bellis, Giovanni Servedio, Vito Walter Anelli, Fedelucio Narducci, Tommaso Di Noia
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have rapidly become central to NLP, demonstrating their ability to adapt to various tasks through prompting techniques, including sentiment analysis. However, we still have a limited understanding of how these models capture sentiment-related information. This study probes the hidden layers of Llama models to pinpoint where sentiment features are most represented and to assess how this affects sentiment analysis. Using probe classifiers, we analyze sentiment encoding across layers and scales, identifying the layers and pooling methods that best capture sentiment signals. Our results show that sentiment information is most concentrated in mid-layers for binary polarity tasks, with detection accuracy increasing up to 14% over prompting techniques. Additionally, we find that in decoder-only models, the last token is not consistently the most informative for sentiment encoding. Finally, this approach enables sentiment tasks to be performed with memory requirements reduced by an average of 57%. These insights contribute to a broader understanding of sentiment in LLMs, suggesting layer-specific probing as an effective approach for sentiment tasks beyond prompting, with potential to enhance model utility and reduce memory requirements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LLaMAs Have Feelings Too: Unveiling Sentiment and Emotion Representations in LLaMA Models Through Probing" investigates how sentiment and emotion information is encoded within the hidden layers of Llama models. The authors train probe classifiers on intermediate representations of Llama models to identify the layers and pooling methods that best capture sentiment signals.  They find that sentiment concepts are most detectable in mid-layers and that concatenating mean, max and min activations are often the most effective pooling methods. They further show that the last token does not consistently yield the best results for probe training.  The paper then introduces SENTRILLAMA, a task-specific adaptation of Llama that uses a reduced set of layers and replaces the language modeling head with a classification head, aiming to reduce computational costs while maintaining performance. The study concludes that sentiment concepts can be linearly detected and internal emotion in certain internal layers and highlights the benefits of SentriLlama in terms of speed and memory usage.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using probing to analyze sentiment representation within LLMs isn't entirely novel. Previous studies have explored probing for various other concepts like truthfulness, factual knowledge, and toxicity. However, this paper offers a systematic investigation of *sentiment* specifically within Llama models, and it introduces SentriLlama, which presents a somewhat new adaptation. The use of various pooling methods and their careful analysis adds a new dimension.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it provides insights into how sentiment is represented in LLMs, helping us understand their internal mechanisms. This understanding can lead to more efficient and controllable sentiment analysis systems. SentriLlama directly addresses the practical problem of LLM efficiency by reducing parameter size and increasing speed while maintaining accuracy. The potential impact lies in enabling sentiment analysis with lower resource requirements.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a well-structured experimental design, systematically comparing different layers, pooling methods, and classifiers.
    *   The introduction of SentriLlama shows the practicality of the findings.
    *   The paper offers a comprehensive analysis of efficiency gains with SentriLlama.
    *   The paper provides a clearly documented methodology, and the code is made available, contributing to reproducibility.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While comparing favorably to instructed LLMs, the models still don't perform as well as DeBERTa or RoBERTa in some cases. This suggests that the "discarded" layers still hold valuable information.
    *   The study is limited to Llama models. While these are important, it needs more generalizability.
    *   The dependence of SentriLlama on layers preceding the optimal one is a potential limitation, as the authors themselves acknowledge.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper could influence future research on efficient LLM adaptation for specific tasks, especially those related to sentiment and emotion. The finding about the importance of middle layers and various pooling methods may guide the development of more compact and targeted models. SentriLlama could be a useful starting point for researchers interested in resource-constrained sentiment analysis.

*   **Justification of Score:** The study offers a valuable, methodical exploration of sentiment representation in LLMs, provides concrete strategies for enhancement, and presents a promising approach to building efficient task-specific adaptations. However, the models investigated perform worse compared to already existing methodologies.  Taking all these factors into consideration, a score of 6 is assigned.
**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### ALTo: Adaptive-Length Tokenizer for Autoregressive Mask Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16495v1)
- **Authors**: Lingfeng Wang, Hualing Lin, Senda Chen, Tao Wang, Changxu Cheng, Yangyang Zhong, Dong Zheng, Wuyue Zhao
- **Abstract**: While humans effortlessly draw visual objects and shapes by adaptively allocating attention based on their complexity, existing multimodal large language models (MLLMs) remain constrained by rigid token representations. Bridging this gap, we propose ALTo, an adaptive length tokenizer for autoregressive mask generation. To achieve this, a novel token length predictor is designed, along with a length regularization term and a differentiable token chunking strategy. We further build ALToLLM that seamlessly integrates ALTo into MLLM. Preferences on the trade-offs between mask quality and efficiency is implemented by group relative policy optimization (GRPO). Experiments demonstrate that ALToLLM achieves state-of-the-art performance with adaptive token cost on popular segmentation benchmarks. Code and models are released at https://github.com/yayafengzi/ALToLLM.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ALTo, an adaptive-length tokenizer for autoregressive mask generation in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Unlike existing methods that use fixed-length token sequences or heuristic rules, ALTo allows the model to autonomously determine the number of mask tokens needed based on the complexity of the input mask. This is achieved through a novel Token Length Predictor (TLP), a length regularization term, and a differentiable token chunking strategy. The paper also presents ALTOLLM, which integrates ALTo into an MLLM and uses Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) to balance mask quality and computational efficiency. Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on segmentation benchmarks with adaptive token cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the adaptive-length tokenization for mask generation *within* an autoregressive setting, where the model *autonomously* decides the token length.  This is a significant departure from previous methods that rely on heuristics or fixed lengths. The differentiable token chunking strategy is also a valuable contribution. The integration with GRPO for controlled trade-offs between quality and efficiency strengthens the work.

*   **Significance:** The ability to adaptively allocate tokens based on object complexity offers several advantages: improved efficiency, reduced redundancy, and potentially better representation of complex structures. This could be highly significant for resource-constrained environments or applications that require real-time processing. The state-of-the-art performance on segmentation benchmarks further underscores the impact of this approach. However, the experiments primarily focus on segmentation tasks; broader applicability to other generative tasks should be explored further.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   Technically sound approach with a novel architecture and training strategy.
    *   Strong empirical results demonstrating state-of-the-art performance.
    *   GRPO implementation for balancing mask quality and computational efficiency.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The experiments are largely confined to image segmentation tasks.  While segmentation is a valid application, it remains unclear how readily ALTo generalizes to other image and video generation scenarios, or other modalities beyond masks.
    *   While it's mentioned, a deeper analysis of the computational gains is needed to fully understand the practical benefits of ALTo in real-world scenarios. Specifically, a detailed ablation study should compare the inference time and memory usage of ALTOLLM against the fixed-length baseline and existing SOTA models with larger model sizes.
    *   The paper doesn't discuss the potential failure modes or limitations of the token length predictor.  What types of scenes or objects might cause it to fail, and how might these failures be mitigated?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has a strong potential to influence research in the areas of multimodal learning, autoregressive generation, and efficient representation learning. It is likely to inspire new approaches for adaptive tokenization and resource allocation in generative models. It also opens up a new direction for MLLMs to understand and generate various data with adaptive mask tokens.

**Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable and novel contribution to the field by introducing an effective method for adaptive-length tokenization in autoregressive mask generation. The results are compelling, and the approach appears well-engineered. While the scope is currently limited to segmentation, the underlying principles are likely to be applicable to other domains. However, more concrete detail would solidify the analysis of the paper and potential areas for future research. The model's limitations need to be better understood and its broader applicability still needs verification.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Human-like Semantic Navigation for Autonomous Driving using Knowledge Representation and Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16498v1)
- **Authors**: Augusto Luis Ballardini, Miguel Ángel Sotelo
- **Abstract**: Achieving full automation in self-driving vehicles remains a challenge, especially in dynamic urban environments where navigation requires real-time adaptability. Existing systems struggle to handle navigation plans when faced with unpredictable changes in road layouts, spontaneous detours, or missing map data, due to their heavy reliance on predefined cartographic information. In this work, we explore the use of Large Language Models to generate Answer Set Programming rules by translating informal navigation instructions into structured, logic-based reasoning. ASP provides non-monotonic reasoning, allowing autonomous vehicles to adapt to evolving scenarios without relying on predefined maps. We present an experimental evaluation in which LLMs generate ASP constraints that encode real-world urban driving logic into a formal knowledge representation. By automating the translation of informal navigation instructions into logical rules, our method improves adaptability and explainability in autonomous navigation. Results show that LLM-driven ASP rule generation supports semantic-based decision-making, offering an explainable framework for dynamic navigation planning that aligns closely with how humans communicate navigational intent.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper explores a novel approach to autonomous driving navigation, aiming to overcome the limitations of traditional map-based systems.
