While layer-wise sparsity adaptation has been explored, fine-grained sparsity control at the output dimension level is a significant step forward.  The iterative adjustment guided by quality metrics is also a valuable contribution, providing a principled way to allocate sparsity budgets. This moves beyond simply applying predefined structural constraints.

*   **Significance:** The results presented are quite compelling. The paper demonstrates substantial improvements in perplexity and zero-shot performance at high sparsity ratios (e.g., 80%) compared to strong baselines. The fact that TRIM can be integrated with existing methods like Wanda and OWL significantly enhances its practical impact, allowing for synergistic improvements. The ablation studies and analyses provide valuable insights into why fine-grained sparsity control is crucial and highlight the limitations of uniform sparsity assumptions.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments across multiple LLM families, sizes, and sparsity levels, consistently demonstrating the effectiveness of TRIM.
    *   **Principled Approach:** The iterative sparsity adjustment process, guided by quality metrics, provides a more adaptive and data-driven way to determine sparsity allocation.
    *   **Integration with Existing Methods:** The compatibility with layer-wise pruning strategies enhances the practicality and applicability of TRIM.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The analysis of output dimension sensitivity and importance provides valuable insights into the underlying factors driving the performance improvements.
    *   The paper provides a clear and well written outline of the TRIM algorithm.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Runtime Overhead:** The iterative nature of TRIM introduces some runtime overhead, although the authors claim it is relatively small and parallelized on the GPU. A more detailed analysis of the scalability of TRIM to even larger models and datasets would be beneficial.
    *   **Quality Metric Dependence:** The performance of TRIM is dependent on the chosen quality metric. While the authors perform an ablation study, further exploration of more sophisticated or adaptive quality metrics could potentially lead to even better results.
    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** Although a hyperparameter tuning process for the learning rate is included, more analysis on the impact of each parameter would be beneficial.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of LLM compression.  The ability to achieve higher sparsity levels without significant performance degradation is crucial for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments. The insights into output dimension sensitivity and importance could also inform other pruning and model compression techniques.

*   **Rigour:** The claims within the paper are mostly rigorous and consistent with the results. The limitations within the paper are appropriately acknowledged.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-supported approach to LLM pruning that achieves state-of-the-art results. The core idea of dimension-wise sparsity adaptation is significant, and the empirical evaluation is comprehensive. While some minor limitations exist regarding runtime overhead and quality metric dependence, the overall contribution is substantial and has the potential to significantly impact the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Self-Rewarding Large Vision-Language Models for Optimizing Prompts in Text-to-Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16763v1)
- **Authors**: Hongji Yang, Yucheng Zhou, Wencheng Han, Jianbing Shen
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image models are powerful for producing high-quality images based on given text prompts, but crafting these prompts often requires specialized vocabulary. To address this, existing methods train rewriting models with supervision from large amounts of manually annotated data and trained aesthetic assessment models. To alleviate the dependence on data scale for model training and the biases introduced by trained models, we propose a novel prompt optimization framework, designed to rephrase a simple user prompt into a sophisticated prompt to a text-to-image model. Specifically, we employ the large vision language models (LVLMs) as the solver to rewrite the user prompt, and concurrently, employ LVLMs as a reward model to score the aesthetics and alignment of the images generated by the optimized prompt. Instead of laborious human feedback, we exploit the prior knowledge of the LVLM to provide rewards, i.e., AI feedback. Simultaneously, the solver and the reward model are unified into one model and iterated in reinforcement learning to achieve self-improvement by giving a solution and judging itself. Results on two popular datasets demonstrate that our method outperforms other strong competitors.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel self-rewarding framework for prompt optimization in text-to-image generation.  The key idea is to use Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) as both the prompt rewriter (solver) and the reward model (evaluator). Instead of relying on human-annotated data or pre-trained aesthetic assessment models, the framework leverages the prior knowledge embedded within the LVLM itself to provide AI feedback.  The LVLM is fine-tuned to rewrite a user's simple prompt into a more sophisticated one suitable for a text-to-image model (like Stable Diffusion). Simultaneously, the LVLM evaluates the aesthetics and alignment of the generated images, providing a reward signal.  The solver and reward model are unified into a single LVLM and iteratively trained through reinforcement learning, achieving self-improvement. Experiments on popular datasets like Beautiful-Prompt and DiffusionDB demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms other prompt optimization techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:**  The concept of a self-rewarding LVLM for prompt optimization is innovative. It moves away from reliance on expensive human annotations and potentially biased pre-trained reward models.  The use of a single model for both rewriting and evaluation simplifies the pipeline.
*   **AI Feedback:** Exploiting LVLMs to provide interpretable AI feedback is a significant contribution, offering a more efficient approach compared to labor-intensive human feedback.
*   **Dynamic Improvement:**  The iterative self-play mechanism allows the reward model to learn and improve *alongside* the prompt rewriting model, addressing a limitation of previous RL-based approaches where the reward model is fixed.
*   **Performance:**  The paper presents strong experimental results, demonstrating superior performance against several competitive baselines on standard datasets.
*   **Practical Significance:**  The approach has the potential to make text-to-image models more accessible to non-experts, improving the quality of generated images from simple prompts.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Demands:** The paper acknowledges that using very large parameter LVLMs (like VILA-40B) for both the solver and evaluator can be computationally expensive, limiting its accessibility.  This is a significant constraint for wider adoption.
*   **Bias in LVLM:** While avoiding reliance on human-annotated aesthetic scores, the framework still inherits the biases present in the pre-trained LVLM itself.  This bias, though "AI feedback," might not reflect human preferences accurately in all cases. A human evaluation is conducted in the paper (Section 4.5) and shows improvements, but the LVLM will introduce particular aesthetic preferences.
*   **Limited Theoretical Justification:**  While the approach is empirically effective, the paper lacks deep theoretical insights into why the particular self-rewarding mechanism and DPO training lead to such improvements. More rigorous theoretical analysis could bolster the claims.
*   **Lack of Ablation Study Depth:** Though the paper does provide an ablation study, it only focuses on different reward modes, and what kind of prompts they accept. The paper provides no ablation study on how the model responds to particular words, i.e., words from section B (Comparisons with common tags.)
*   **Narrow Scope of Evaluation:** The evaluation focuses mainly on aesthetic scores and CLIP scores. While these are relevant metrics, a more comprehensive evaluation involving diverse human evaluators on different tasks would strengthen the paper.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper presents a novel architecture and training paradigm for prompt optimization that is likely to be influential in the field. The concept of the self-rewarding LVLM has broad implications. Using AI itself to judge the AI output moves towards a better understanding of reward functions.

**Overall Score:**

Score: 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel and impactful method with strong empirical results. The idea of self-rewarding LVLMs for prompt optimization is innovative and tackles important challenges in text-to-image generation. However, the method's computational demands, the inherent bias within LVLMs, limited theoretical analysis, and lack of ablation study depth slightly lowers the score. Despite these weaknesses, the approach offers a practical and promising direction, warranting its significance and potential impact on the field of text-to-image generation and prompt engineering.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### When Safety Detectors Aren't Enough: A Stealthy and Effective Jailbreak Attack on LLMs via Steganographic Techniques
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16765v1)
- **Authors**: Jianing Geng, Biao Yi, Zekun Fei, Tongxi Wu, Lihai Nie, Zheli Liu
- **Abstract**: Jailbreak attacks pose a serious threat to large language models (LLMs) by bypassing built-in safety mechanisms and leading to harmful outputs. Studying these attacks is crucial for identifying vulnerabilities and improving model security. This paper presents a systematic survey of jailbreak methods from the novel perspective of stealth. We find that existing attacks struggle to simultaneously achieve toxic stealth (concealing toxic content) and linguistic stealth (maintaining linguistic naturalness). Motivated by this, we propose StegoAttack, a fully stealthy jailbreak attack that uses steganography to hide the harmful query within benign, semantically coherent text. The attack then prompts the LLM to extract the hidden query and respond in an encrypted manner. This approach effectively hides malicious intent while preserving naturalness, allowing it to evade both built-in and external safety mechanisms. We evaluate StegoAttack on four safety-aligned LLMs from major providers, benchmarking against eight state-of-the-art methods. StegoAttack achieves an average attack success rate (ASR) of 92.00%, outperforming the strongest baseline by 11.0%. Its ASR drops by less than 1% even under external detection (e.g., Llama Guard). Moreover, it attains the optimal comprehensive scores on stealth detection metrics, demonstrating both high efficacy and exceptional stealth capabilities. The code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/StegoAttack-Jail66
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces StegoAttack, a novel jailbreak attack on large language models (LLMs) that aims to achieve both toxic stealth (concealing harmful content) and linguistic stealth (maintaining natural language fluency). The attack uses steganography to hide harmful queries within benign text and prompts the LLM to extract and respond to the hidden query in an encrypted manner. The authors evaluate StegoAttack on four safety-aligned LLMs, comparing it against eight state-of-the-art jailbreak methods.  The results show that StegoAttack achieves high attack success rates while effectively evading both built-in and external safety mechanisms, even those like Llama Guard. The paper also includes a systematic classification of existing jailbreak methods from the perspective of stealth and conducts ablation studies to analyze key parameters in the attack process.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's key novelty lies in the application of steganography for jailbreaking LLMs. While existing jailbreak methods have focused on either toxic stealth or linguistic stealth, StegoAttack attempts to combine both in a more robust manner. The use of steganography to mask the harmful query within a seemingly innocuous text, while innovative, builds upon well-established information-hiding techniques.
* **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its demonstration of a highly effective and stealthy jailbreak attack that can bypass current safety mechanisms in LLMs. By highlighting the limitations of existing defenses, it underscores the need for more sophisticated safeguards. The systematic survey of jailbreak methods from the perspective of stealth provides a valuable contribution to understanding the current landscape of attacks.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Effectiveness:** The experimental results clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of StegoAttack in achieving high attack success rates while evading safety detectors.
    * **Stealth:** The paper presents compelling evidence that StegoAttack achieves both toxic and linguistic stealth, making it difficult to detect using current methods.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation includes a comparison with a wide range of state-of-the-art jailbreak methods and a thorough analysis of the attack's parameters.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the methodology, results, and analysis. The figures are informative and help to illustrate the key concepts.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The steganographic approach increases the attack's complexity compared to simpler prompt-based jailbreaks. This complexity might limit its accessibility to less technically skilled attackers.
    * **Limited Generalization:** The evaluation primarily focuses on a specific set of LLMs. While the results are promising, further evaluation on a wider range of models is needed to assess the generalizability of StegoAttack. The choice of models (GPT-03, Llama4, DeepSeek-R1, and QwQ-32B) seems unusual, given that GPT-3 (which GPT-03 presumably refers to) is a somewhat outdated model. It's unclear if GPT-4 was used, or if a typo was made. Similarly, it may be preferable to evaluate Llama 2 or Llama 3 models over Llama 4.
    * **Reliance on Prompt Engineering:** The effectiveness of StegoAttack relies heavily on prompt engineering, both for generating the steganographic text and for guiding the LLM to extract and respond to the hidden query. While the paper explores some aspects of prompt optimization, further research is needed to understand the optimal prompt design strategies.
    * **Potential Defenses:** The paper acknowledges the potential for defenses against StegoAttack, such as improved safety detectors and better-governed language models, and it would have been beneficial to discuss the potential limitations or circumvention strategies that could be implemented with LLMs in practice to counter this attack vector.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by introducing a new, stealthy jailbreak attack based on steganography.  However, the complexity of the attack and its reliance on prompt engineering, coupled with the limited generalizability of the evaluation, temper its overall impact. While the experimental results are compelling, more extensive testing and analysis are needed to fully understand the attack's capabilities and limitations. The somewhat dated choice of LLM test models further reduces the score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### IFEval-Audio: Benchmarking Instruction-Following Capability in Audio-based Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16774v1)
- **Authors**: Yiming Gao, Bin Wang, Chengwei Wei, Shuo Sun, AiTi Aw
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong instruction-following capabilities in text-based tasks. However, this ability often deteriorates in multimodal models after alignment with non-text modalities such as images or audio. While several recent efforts have investigated instruction-following performance in text and vision-language models, instruction-following in audio-based large language models remains largely unexplored. To bridge this gap, we introduce IFEval-Audio, a novel evaluation dataset designed to assess the ability to follow instructions in an audio LLM. IFEval-Audio contains 280 audio-instruction-answer triples across six diverse dimensions: Content, Capitalization, Symbol, List Structure, Length, and Format. Each example pairs an audio input with a text instruction, requiring the model to generate an output that follows a specified structure. We benchmark state-of-the-art audio LLMs on their ability to follow audio-involved instructions. The dataset is released publicly to support future research in this emerging area.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "IFEval-Audio: Benchmarking Instruction-Following Capability in Audio-based Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces IFEval-Audio, a novel dataset designed to evaluate the instruction-following capabilities of audio-based Large Language Models (LLMs). The dataset comprises 280 audio-instruction-answer triples across six dimensions: Content, Capitalization, Symbol, List Structure, Length, and Format.  It utilizes audio from diverse sources (speech, music, environmental sounds) paired with text instructions that require models to generate specific text outputs.  The authors benchmark several state-of-the-art audio LLMs on IFEval-Audio, highlighting the strengths and weaknesses of each model in following different types of instructions. They make the dataset publicly available to encourage further research in this relatively unexplored area. The evaluation framework uses both rule-based and LLM-based scoring to assess format adherence and semantic correctness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a significant gap in the field of multimodal LLMs. While instruction-following has been extensively studied for text and images, it remains largely unexplored for audio. IFEval-Audio is a pioneering dataset specifically designed for evaluating audio-based instruction following.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The dataset is well-structured across six diverse dimensions that comprehensively assess instruction-following abilities. The use of both rule-based scoring for format and LLM-based scoring for semantics is a strength, providing a more complete picture of model performance.
*   **Dataset Diversity:**  The incorporation of audio from different sources (speech, music, and environmental sounds) enhances the generalizability of the benchmark and ensures that models are tested on a variety of audio inputs.
*   **Public Availability:** The public release of the IFEval-Audio dataset is a significant contribution, enabling other researchers to build upon their work and accelerate progress in the field.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the design and construction of the dataset, the evaluation methodology, and the experimental results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dataset Size:** The dataset size (280 triples) is relatively small compared to many text-based benchmarks. While it is a good starting point, a larger dataset would likely provide a more robust evaluation.
*   **Language Limitation:** The dataset is English-only, limiting its applicability to multilingual audio instruction-following.
*   **Reliance on Judge Models:** The use of LLMs to evaluate semantic correctness introduces a potential bias, as the evaluation is dependent on the capabilities and biases of the judge model (LLaMA 2, in this case).
*   **Limited Baselines:** While the paper benchmarks several models, the field of audio LLMs is rapidly evolving. More comprehensive experiments including SOTA text-audio models would provide a more exhaustive and comparative analysis.
*   **Potential for Overfitting:** Given the relatively small dataset size, there is a risk that models could be specifically fine-tuned to perform well on IFEval-Audio, potentially reducing its effectiveness as a benchmark over time.

**Significance:**

The paper is significant because it highlights the importance of audio instruction-following, a key area for future development in multimodal LLMs. IFEval-Audio provides a valuable tool for researchers to assess and improve the performance of audio-based LLMs.  It encourages a more nuanced understanding of model capabilities beyond simple format adherence and promotes progress toward models that can generate semantically correct and contextually relevant responses based on audio input. The dataset could be used to inform the development of new audio-language model architectures, training strategies, and evaluation methods.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's strengths (novelty, comprehensive evaluation, public availability) outweigh its weaknesses (small dataset size, language limitation, reliance on a judge model).  While the dataset is not without limitations, it addresses a critical gap in the field and offers a solid foundation for future research. It pioneers a benchmark for evaluating audio models on instruction following and provides valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reasoning Beyond Language: A Comprehensive Survey on Latent Chain-of-Thought Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16782v1)
- **Authors**: Xinghao Chen, Anhao Zhao, Heming Xia, Xuan Lu, Hanlin Wang, Yanjun Chen, Wei Zhang, Jian Wang, Wenjie Li, Xiaoyu Shen
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved impressive performance on complex reasoning tasks with Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting. However, conventional CoT relies on reasoning steps explicitly verbalized in natural language, introducing inefficiencies and limiting its applicability to abstract reasoning. To address this, there has been growing research interest in latent CoT reasoning, where inference occurs within latent spaces. By decoupling reasoning from language, latent reasoning promises richer cognitive representations and more flexible, faster inference. Researchers have explored various directions in this promising field, including training methodologies, structural innovations, and internal reasoning mechanisms. This paper presents a comprehensive overview and analysis of this reasoning paradigm. We begin by proposing a unified taxonomy from four perspectives: token-wise strategies, internal mechanisms, analysis, and applications. We then provide in-depth discussions and comparative analyses of representative methods, highlighting their design patterns, strengths, and open challenges. We aim to provide a structured foundation for advancing this emerging direction in LLM reasoning. The relevant papers will be regularly updated at https://github.com/EIT-NLP/Awesome-Latent-CoT.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided document, which is the first draft of a survey paper on "Latent Chain-of-Thought Reasoning." Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary**

The paper provides a comprehensive survey of latent chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning in Large Language Models (LLMs). It argues that while traditional CoT prompting relies on explicit, natural language reasoning steps, latent CoT moves reasoning into latent spaces, potentially offering greater efficiency, flexibility, and the ability to represent more abstract cognitive processes. The survey proposes a taxonomy of latent CoT methods based on four perspectives: token-wise strategies, internal mechanisms, analysis and applications. It then analyzes representative methods within each category, comparing their training strategies, design patterns, supervision signals, and efficiency trade-offs. Finally, the paper identifies open challenges and research frontiers in this emerging field.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Timeliness and Relevance:** Latent CoT is a relatively new and rapidly developing area within LLM research. A comprehensive survey is valuable for researchers to understand the landscape, consolidate knowledge, and identify promising directions.
    *   **Comprehensive Taxonomy:** The proposed taxonomy provides a structured framework for understanding and classifying different latent CoT approaches. The taxonomy is based on clear criteria (token-wise strategies vs. internal mechanisms; discrete tokens vs continuous tokens).
    *   **In-Depth Analysis:** The paper doesn't just list methods but provides comparative analyses, discussing the strengths and weaknesses of different approaches, including training difficulties and generalization issues.
    *   **Clear Identification of Challenges and Future Directions:** The section on challenges and future directions is crucial for guiding future research efforts. The paper identifies important issues such as training difficulties, generalization issues, and interpretability concerns.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Heavily Descriptive:** While the survey offers a good organization and analysis of existing methods, it could benefit from more synthesis and critical comparison, especially in identifying the most promising future directions. What approaches are the "most" promising, according to what metrics?
    *   **Limited Novelty in Content:** The paper focuses on summarizing existing work. While valuable, the core novelty rests on the taxonomy and analysis, and the analysis could be more substantive. The paper doesn't present any new theoretical insights or empirical results that would significantly advance the field beyond summarizing the state of the art.
    *   **Evaluation focus**: While the survey talks about the applications, the analysis section doesn't seem to explore in-depth the applicability of each of these different CoT variants.
*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    The novelty of this work primarily stems from the compilation, categorization, and early analysis of the work on latent CoT. It provides a useful service to the community, but falls short of offering breakthroughs. There is some discussion regarding the limitations of existing approaches (evaluation gap, alignment risks), but the discussion is limited. The paper's significance relies on its ability to shape future research directions.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of 7.

*   The timeliness and comprehensiveness of the survey contribute significantly to its value. A clear taxonomy and comparative analysis of approaches will be helpful for researchers in the field.
*   However, the survey primarily synthesizes existing knowledge, with limited novel insights or empirical investigations. Its impact will depend on how effectively it guides future research. A stronger analysis, more detailed evaluations and more critical discussions would increase the overall value of the submission.
*   The level of critical thinking displayed in the paper, while commendable, isn't enough to elevate the score. The paper doesn't showcase a very deep thinking about what is missing or what must happen to enable future breakthroughs.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### CoTSRF: Utilize Chain of Thought as Stealthy and Robust Fingerprint of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16785v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenzhen Ren, GuoBiao Li, Sheng Li, Zhenxing Qian, Xinpeng Zhang
- **Abstract**: Despite providing superior performance, open-source large language models (LLMs) are vulnerable to abusive usage. To address this issue, recent works propose LLM fingerprinting methods to identify the specific source LLMs behind suspect applications. However, these methods fail to provide stealthy and robust fingerprint verification. In this paper, we propose a novel LLM fingerprinting scheme, namely CoTSRF, which utilizes the Chain of Thought (CoT) as the fingerprint of an LLM. CoTSRF first collects the responses from the source LLM by querying it with crafted CoT queries. Then, it applies contrastive learning to train a CoT extractor that extracts the CoT feature (i.e., fingerprint) from the responses. Finally, CoTSRF conducts fingerprint verification by comparing the Kullback-Leibler divergence between the CoT features of the source and suspect LLMs against an empirical threshold. Various experiments have been conducted to demonstrate the advantage of our proposed CoTSRF for fingerprinting LLMs, particularly in stealthy and robust fingerprint verification.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper, along with a justified novelty/significance score.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CoTSRF, a novel fingerprinting scheme for large language models (LLMs) that leverages the Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning process as a stealthy and robust fingerprint.  The method involves crafting CoT queries, collecting responses from the target LLM (source and suspect), training a CoT extractor using contrastive learning to capture the underlying reasoning patterns, and then using Kullback-Leibler divergence to compare the CoT features and verify if the suspect LLM is an infringing copy of the source LLM. The authors also introduce a High-Temperature Data Augmentation (HTDA) strategy to increase the diversity of responses during training. Experiments are conducted to demonstrate the effectiveness, reliability, stealthiness, and robustness of CoTSRF against various attacks, including output perturbation and fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The primary strength of the paper lies in its innovative use of Chain-of-Thought as a fingerprint for LLMs. While prior works have explored model fingerprinting, utilizing CoT for this purpose is a relatively unexplored avenue, particularly in the context of black-box access.
    *   **Robustness:**  The paper demonstrates through experimentation that CoTSRF is reasonably robust against output perturbation and fine-tuning attacks.  This is a significant advantage over some existing fingerprinting methods that are easily disrupted by such manipulations. The HTDA strategy appears to contribute to this robustness by introducing variance into the training data.
    *   **Stealthiness:**  The paper presents a compelling argument for the stealthiness of CoTSRF based on perplexity scores, suggesting that the crafted queries are less likely to be flagged by prompt filtering mechanisms compared to methods that use adversarial prefixes or other less semantically coherent inputs.
    *   **Black-box applicability:** The method is designed for black-box access scenarios, which are common in real-world API deployments. This practical focus is a major strength.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The experiments seem comprehensive, covering effectiveness, reliability (using unseen benign models), stealthiness, and robustness against different attack vectors.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of LLMs Tested:** The evaluation focuses on a relatively small number of open-source LLMs, primarily around the 7B parameter scale. While these models are representative, the generalizability of CoTSRF to larger and more diverse LLM architectures (e.g., models with hundreds of billions of parameters, Mixture-of-Experts architectures) is not definitively established.
    *   **Dependence on CoT:** The fingerprint relies entirely on the CoT reasoning patterns of LLMs. While this is a novel approach, it also means that the effectiveness is inherently tied to the consistency and distinctiveness of these patterns across different models. Models that have been heavily fine-tuned to significantly alter their reasoning process might pose a challenge. Furthermore, if LLM architectures evolve to use significantly different reasoning methods beyond CoT, the approach might become less effective.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity and Threshold Selection:** The method involves several parameters, including the temperature in HTDA, the margin in the triplet loss, and the KL divergence threshold. The paper provides empirical values for these parameters, but a more detailed analysis of their sensitivity and a systematic approach to threshold selection would strengthen the work. How consistent this threshold is among different base models, or if it requires extensive tuning across models, would also be important.
    *   **Lack of Comparison with More Recent Watermarking Methods:** While the paper mentions watermarking approaches, the evaluation doesn't include a direct empirical comparison against recent state-of-the-art black-box LLM watermarking techniques.  Including such a comparison would help to better contextualize the performance of CoTSRF.
    *   **Theoretical Analysis:** The paper could benefit from a more rigorous theoretical analysis of why CoT patterns are unique to specific LLMs and how robust they are to different types of model manipulation. Some recent works have indicated that CoT reasoning may be heavily influenced by data contamination in the training data, rather than inherent architecture differences.

*   **Significance and Potential Impact:**

    *   The CoT-based fingerprinting approach is a significant contribution to the field of LLM security and copyright protection. As LLMs become more prevalent, ensuring their responsible use and preventing unauthorized replication is crucial. CoTSRF provides a viable technique for addressing these challenges.
    *   The HTDA strategy and the contrastive learning framework for training the CoT extractor are valuable techniques that can be applied to other LLM fingerprinting or watermarking methods.
    *   The findings of this research could influence the development of more robust and stealthy LLM fingerprinting techniques and contribute to the broader effort of securing and protecting LLMs.

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and promising approach to LLM fingerprinting. The use of CoT as a fingerprint is innovative, and the experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness, stealthiness, and robustness of CoTSRF. However, the evaluation is somewhat limited in scope, and certain aspects (parameter sensitivity, theoretical analysis, comparison against more recent watermarking methods) could be further strengthened. While the paper makes a solid contribution, it is not a breakthrough result that fundamentally changes the landscape of LLM security, but rather a valuable addition that opens up new avenues for research and development. The caveats regarding CoT reasoning being influenced by training data, and the generalizability across different parameter LLM scales is an important point to address.

I am setting the score at 6.5 because it represents a reasonably good contribution, but has enough significant weakness to not warrant a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Accidental Misalignment: Fine-Tuning Language Models Induces Unexpected Vulnerability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16789v1)
- **Authors**: Punya Syon Pandey, Samuel Simko, Kellin Pelrine, Zhijing Jin
- **Abstract**: As large language models gain popularity, their vulnerability to adversarial attacks remains a primary concern. While fine-tuning models on domain-specific datasets is often employed to improve model performance, it can introduce vulnerabilities within the underlying model. In this work, we investigate Accidental Misalignment, unexpected vulnerabilities arising from characteristics of fine-tuning data. We begin by identifying potential correlation factors such as linguistic features, semantic similarity, and toxicity within our experimental datasets. We then evaluate the adversarial performance of these fine-tuned models and assess how dataset factors correlate with attack success rates. Lastly, we explore potential causal links, offering new insights into adversarial defense strategies and highlighting the crucial role of dataset design in preserving model alignment. Our code is available at https://github.com/psyonp/accidental_misalignment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Accidental Misalignment: Fine-Tuning Language Models Induces Unexpected Vulnerability" investigates how fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) on seemingly benign domain-specific datasets can inadvertently introduce vulnerabilities, making them more susceptible to adversarial attacks.  The authors explore the relationship between dataset characteristics (linguistic features, semantic similarity, toxicity, etc.) and a model's vulnerability after fine-tuning. They fine-tune LLMs on a variety of datasets (benign, cybersecurity, legal, harmful), evaluate their performance against jailbreaking attacks, correlate dataset features with attack success rates, and perform feature intervention experiments. The key findings suggest that certain dataset characteristics like response token length and toxicity positively correlate with increased vulnerability.  The feature ablation experiments provide preliminary evidence that interventions minimizing identified dataset features can improve model alignment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper tackles an important issue: the unintended consequences of fine-tuning LLMs. While previous work has shown that fine-tuning can erode safety, this paper attempts to systematically analyze *which* dataset factors contribute to the problem. This is a valuable step beyond simply observing the phenomenon. However, this space has been explored before; the novelty is partially diminished by its similarity to the general concepts of representation and gradient matching. The feature isolation experiments are also good, but the paper would be stronger by including more diverse ablations in conjunction with other features.

*   **Significance:** The findings have practical implications for how LLMs are fine-tuned and deployed. Understanding the dataset factors that contribute to misalignment could inform the creation of safer fine-tuning procedures and adversarial defense strategies. The work also highlights the importance of careful dataset design in preserving model alignment.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper's methodology is reasonably sound. The use of a variety of datasets, attacks, and metrics strengthens the analysis.
    *   The identification of correlations between dataset features (e.g., response token length, toxicity) and attack success rates is a valuable contribution.
    *   The feature isolation experiments, while preliminary, provide supporting evidence for the role of dataset characteristics in model misalignment.
    *   The writing is generally clear.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The correlational nature of the results limits causal claims. While the feature ablation provides some evidence for causation, the limited scope makes it difficult to draw strong conclusions. The study's conclusion that "interventions minimizing identified dataset features can improve model alignment" is weakened by the number of experiments undertaken on each dataset.
    *   The ablation study only ablated the top and bottom 20%, which leaves a lot of room for improvement to better solidify the results.
    *   The generalizability study (using only one attack and two other models) could have been more comprehensive and rigorous.
    *   Some of the correlations, while statistically significant, may have limited practical significance. The magnitude of the correlation coefficients should be considered.
    *   The paper could be strengthened by exploring potential confounders and alternative explanations for the observed correlations.
    *   The choice of metrics (e.g., those for semantic similarity) and analysis methods could be better justified.
    *   There are very few qualitative examples. If the dataset contained any sensitive material, a few carefully chosen examples would greatly improve the paper's impact.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence research in adversarial robustness, dataset design, and fine-tuning of LLMs. It could inspire further investigations into the causal mechanisms underlying accidental misalignment and the development of more robust fine-tuning techniques. The paper may influence the process of developing future AI models.

**Rigorous Rationale:**
The paper's strength lies in its systematic investigation of a real-world problem. However, the limitations regarding causal claims, the scope of the experiments, and the generalizability study constrain its impact. Although the findings are interesting, the paper will only be impactful if it inspires a more rigorous study on the subject.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Learning Flexible Forward Trajectories for Masked Molecular Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16790v1)
- **Authors**: Hyunjin Seo, Taewon Kim, Sihyun Yu, SungSoo Ahn
- **Abstract**: Masked diffusion models (MDMs) have achieved notable progress in modeling discrete data, while their potential in molecular generation remains underexplored. In this work, we explore their potential and introduce the surprising result that naively applying standards MDMs severely degrades the performance. We identify the critical cause of this issue as a state-clashing problem-where the forward diffusion of distinct molecules collapse into a common state, resulting in a mixture of reconstruction targets that cannot be learned using typical reverse diffusion process with unimodal predictions. To mitigate this, we propose Masked Element-wise Learnable Diffusion (MELD) that orchestrates per-element corruption trajectories to avoid collision between distinct molecular graphs. This is achieved through a parameterized noise scheduling network that assigns distinct corruption rates to individual graph elements, i.e., atoms and bonds. Extensive experiments on diverse molecular benchmarks reveal that MELD markedly enhances overall generation quality compared to element-agnostic noise scheduling, increasing the chemical validity of vanilla MDMs on ZINC250K from 15% to 93%, Furthermore, it achieves state-of-the-art property alignment in conditional generation tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the under-explored potential of Masked Diffusion Models (MDMs) in molecular generation. It identifies a critical limitation: a "state-clashing" problem, where forward diffusion causes distinct molecules to converge to a common, unlearnable state, leading to poor performance. The authors propose Masked Element-wise Learnable Diffusion (MELD), a novel MDM variant that mitigates state-clashing by orchestrating per-element (atom/bond) corruption trajectories using a parameterized noise scheduling network. The network learns distinct corruption rates for each element, preventing collision between distinct molecular graphs during the forward diffusion process.  Experiments on various molecular benchmarks (QM9, Polymers, ZINC250K) show that MELD significantly improves generation quality compared to element-agnostic MDMs and achieves state-of-the-art property alignment in conditional generation tasks. The authors also provide analysis revealing the unmasking preferences of the denoising module and the diversity observed in the later phases of the learned noise schedule.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The identification of the "state-clashing" problem in applying MDMs to molecular graphs is a key contribution. While MDMs have been successful in other domains, the authors pinpoint a specific challenge in the molecular context due to the discrete, graph-structured nature of the data and the standard MDM's element-agnostic noise scheduling. The MELD approach to address this is also novel. The introduction of a learnable, element-wise noise schedule is a clever way to guide the forward diffusion process and reduce state collision. The paper’s novelty stems not only from the proposed architecture but also from identifying the underlying issue that limits the applicability of standard MDMs to this specific task.
*   **Significance:** The state-clashing issue significantly hinders applying MDMs in the molecular generation domain. MELD is the first to recognize the issue and mitigate it by learning per-element noise schedules. By dramatically improving the chemical validity and property alignment of generated molecules, MELD paves the way for MDMs to be more effective in molecular design tasks. The experimental results are compelling, showcasing substantial improvements over baselines, including standard MDMs and other molecular generation methods. The ablations further solidify the impact of the element-wise learnable noise schedule. The analysis also offers insights into the model's behavior and confirms the initial state-clashing hypothesis. The improvement is significant, making it possible to use masked diffusion models where they were previously impractical.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper articulates the state-clashing problem very well with figures that help explain the issue.
    *   **Elegant solution:** The element-wise learnable noise schedule is a simple but effective solution.
    *   **Strong experimental results:** The results across diverse benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of MELD.
    *   **Insightful analysis:** The ablation studies and qualitative analysis provide a deeper understanding of the model's behavior.
    * The study is a complete, from identifying the issue to resolving it.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper addresses the "state-clashing" problem, more work is needed to avoid multimodality when a large fraction of molecules is masked at the latest diffusion stages.
    * While the MELD method achieves robust performance in molecular generation tasks, there is a need to focus on ways of reducing high training complexity, given its need for joint optimization of the forward process and the reverse process.
    * The paper could delve deeper into the limitations, discussing potential failure modes or scenarios where MELD might not perform as well.

* **Potential Impact**
The proposed research could potentially improve molecular discovery by allowing for the generation of more realistic compounds, with specific pre-tuned characteristics that satisfy target conditions.

* Justification:
The paper makes a substantial contribution to the area of molecular design by addressing the issue of state-clashing, which has been hindering the use of MDMs in molecular generation. The MELD architecture solves this issue effectively, by achieving significant improvements in validity, property alignment, and several other experimental metrics.

**Score: 8**

While MELD is a significant advancement, and opens the door to MDMs in molecular generation, further research is needed to resolve all of its known limitations. For example, while the study provides significant results, addressing more complex molecules may need to focus on ways of further reducing training complexity. The contribution is novel and has a high potential for impact, but there is a small room for further refinement and optimization.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### REPA Works Until It Doesn't: Early-Stopped, Holistic Alignment Supercharges Diffusion Training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16792v1)
- **Authors**: Ziqiao Wang, Wangbo Zhao, Yuhao Zhou, Zekai Li, Zhiyuan Liang, Mingjia Shi, Xuanlei Zhao, Pengfei Zhou, Kaipeng Zhang, Zhangyang Wang, Kai Wang, Yang You
- **Abstract**: Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) deliver state-of-the-art image quality, yet their training remains notoriously slow. A recent remedy -- representation alignment (REPA) that matches DiT hidden features to those of a non-generative teacher (e.g. DINO) -- dramatically accelerates the early epochs but plateaus or even degrades performance later. We trace this failure to a capacity mismatch: once the generative student begins modelling the joint data distribution, the teacher's lower-dimensional embeddings and attention patterns become a straitjacket rather than a guide. We then introduce HASTE (Holistic Alignment with Stage-wise Termination for Efficient training), a two-phase schedule that keeps the help and drops the hindrance. Phase I applies a holistic alignment loss that simultaneously distills attention maps (relational priors) and feature projections (semantic anchors) from the teacher into mid-level layers of the DiT, yielding rapid convergence. Phase II then performs one-shot termination that deactivates the alignment loss, once a simple trigger such as a fixed iteration is hit, freeing the DiT to focus on denoising and exploit its generative capacity. HASTE speeds up training of diverse DiTs without architecture changes. On ImageNet 256X256, it reaches the vanilla SiT-XL/2 baseline FID in 50 epochs and matches REPA's best FID in 500 epochs, amounting to a 28X reduction in optimization steps. HASTE also improves text-to-image DiTs on MS-COCO, demonstrating to be a simple yet principled recipe for efficient diffusion training across various tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/NUS-HPC-AI-Lab/HASTE .
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "REPA Works Until It Doesn't: Early-Stopped, Holistic Alignment Supercharges Diffusion Training" addresses the slow training of Diffusion Transformers (DiTs). It identifies a key limitation of the REPA (Representation Alignment) technique, where aligning DiT features with a fixed teacher network initially accelerates training but later plateaus or degrades performance due to a capacity mismatch. The authors propose HASTE (Holistic Alignment with Stage-wise Termination for Efficient training), a two-phase training schedule. Phase I applies a holistic alignment loss (features *and* attention maps) from a teacher (DINOv2), and Phase II terminates the alignment loss after a trigger, allowing the DiT to focus on denoising and its generative capacity. HASTE achieves significant speedups on ImageNet and MS-COCO without architectural changes, improving training efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a novel and important insight: that representation alignment, while initially helpful, becomes detrimental due to the student outgrowing the teacher. This is a crucial observation previously less emphasized in the literature. The proposed HASTE is a simple but effective solution built on this observation. The dual-channel alignment (feature + attention) is also a worthwhile addition, even if less transformative on its own.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it directly tackles a major bottleneck in diffusion model training: computational cost. Accelerating DiT training *without* architectural changes makes the method broadly applicable. The experiments thoroughly validate HASTE's effectiveness across different DiT architectures and datasets, providing strong evidence of its practical utility. The gradient-angle analysis used to diagnose the problem is a valuable technique that could be applied in other contexts as well.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper begins with a clear statement of the problem (slow DiT training) and identifies a specific limitation of an existing solution (REPA).
    *   **Convincing Diagnosis:** The "capacity mismatch" hypothesis is well-supported by the gradient-angle analysis and the control experiment using low-frequency teacher inputs.
    *   **Simple and Effective Solution:** HASTE is easy to implement (two lines of code) and doesn't require specialized kernels or extensive hyperparameter tuning, making it practically valuable.
    *   **Thorough Experimental Validation:** The experiments are well-designed and demonstrate HASTE's benefits across various datasets, models, and settings (with/without classifier-free guidance). Ablation studies effectively isolate the contributions of different components.
    *   **Comprehensive Visualizations:** The generated image comparisons are insightful and showcase the improvements in visual quality at early training stages.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited novelty in attention distillation:** The use of attention distillation isn't groundbreaking in itself, as it has been explored in other contexts, particularly in vision transformers. However, the integration of attention distillation within the REPA framework and its combination with the stage-wise termination is novel.
    *   **The termination criteria exploration could be expanded:** The paper mainly explores fixed iteration termination. While gradient-based termination is mentioned, more extensive exploration of adaptive termination strategies could further improve performance and robustness.
    *   **The choice of teacher model seems fixed** While DINOv2 is a popular choice, the paper lacks a discussion of how the choice of teacher model impacts the effectiveness of HASTE. The nature of HASTE being beneficial because of a mismatch begs the question of the ideal model to use.

*   **Potential Impact:** HASTE is likely to be widely adopted by the diffusion model community due to its simplicity, effectiveness, and broad applicability. It has the potential to significantly reduce the computational cost of training DiTs, making them more accessible to researchers and practitioners with limited resources. The insights about representation alignment and the diagnostic techniques used in the paper could also influence future research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a strong contribution to the field of diffusion model training. It identifies a critical limitation of an existing technique, provides a clear explanation for the limitation, and proposes a simple yet effective solution that is thoroughly validated through experiments. The novelty lies in the recognition of the dynamic interaction between student and teacher models during training and the adaptive adjustment made to exploit this behavior. Though some components of the method have roots in prior work, their combination and application within the specific context of diffusion model training constitute a significant advance. It's highly likely that this paper will be heavily cited and its methods will be incorporated into standard training pipelines for diffusion models.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### SEED: Speaker Embedding Enhancement Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16798v1)
- **Authors**: KiHyun Nam, Jungwoo Heo, Jee-weon Jung, Gangin Park, Chaeyoung Jung, Ha-Jin Yu, Joon Son Chung
- **Abstract**: A primary challenge when deploying speaker recognition systems in real-world applications is performance degradation caused by environmental mismatch. We propose a diffusion-based method that takes speaker embeddings extracted from a pre-trained speaker recognition model and generates refined embeddings. For training, our approach progressively adds Gaussian noise to both clean and noisy speaker embeddings extracted from clean and noisy speech, respectively, via forward process of a diffusion model, and then reconstructs them to clean embeddings in the reverse process. While inferencing, all embeddings are regenerated via diffusion process. Our method needs neither speaker label nor any modification to the existing speaker recognition pipeline. Experiments on evaluation sets simulating environment mismatch scenarios show that our method can improve recognition accuracy by up to 19.6% over baseline models while retaining performance on conventional scenarios. We publish our code here https://github.com/kaistmm/seed-pytorch
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SEED (Speaker Embedding Enhancement Diffusion), a novel diffusion-based method to enhance speaker embeddings extracted from noisy speech. SEED aims to improve the robustness of speaker recognition systems against environmental mismatch by progressively adding Gaussian noise to both clean and noisy speaker embeddings and then reconstructing them back to a clean representation.  This process reduces the discrepancy between embeddings from clean and noisy audio, allowing the system to better recognize speakers even in adverse conditions. The key benefits highlighted are that it doesn't require speaker labels, doesn't modify the existing speaker recognition pipeline significantly, and shows promising results in mitigating environment mismatch.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in applying diffusion models *directly* to speaker embeddings, rather than using them solely for front-end audio enhancement.  Previous DPM approaches in speech primarily focused on cleaning audio signals. Applying DPM at the embedding level to refine speaker representations is a fresh perspective. The idea of leveraging the shared speaker identity between clean and noisy embeddings as a basis for learning is also interesting. While diffusion models are not new *per se*, their application within the speaker recognition domain, specifically at the embedding level, presents an incremental yet valuable advancement.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a critical challenge in real-world speaker recognition deployments: environmental mismatch. The paper shows compelling improvements in accuracy in mismatched conditions (up to 19.6%), suggesting that SEED can substantially enhance the usability of speaker recognition systems in noisy environments. The practicality of the method, requiring minimal changes to existing systems and no speaker labels for training, further increases its potential impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel Application of DPMs:** Applies DPMs at the speaker embedding level, a relatively unexplored area.
    *   **Practical and Lightweight:** Doesn't require extensive retraining or modification of the speaker recognition pipeline.
    *   **Label-Free Training:** Avoids the need for speaker labels, allowing the use of large, readily available clean speech datasets.
    *   **Performance Gains:** Demonstrates significant improvements in accuracy under mismatched conditions.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the method and experimental setup.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Nature:**  While novel in application, the underlying diffusion model technology is well-established.
    *   **Dependence on Pre-trained Model:** The method relies on a pre-trained speaker recognition model, making it sensitive to the quality of that model. The approach does not address imperfections in the pre-trained feature extractor itself.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies seem limited. More analysis to understand the impact of different components of SEED (e.g., different noise schedules, number of diffusion steps) would strengthen the paper.
    *  **Training Stability:** The paper acknowledges training instability due to the DDPM mechanism. Addressing this more explicitly would be beneficial. How sensitive is SEED to hyperparameter selection or initialization?

*   **Potential Impact:**  The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging further research into embedding-level enhancement techniques using generative models. It offers a practical and effective solution to address environmental mismatch in speaker recognition, potentially leading to improved real-world performance. The fact that it does not require speaker labels is also highly practical.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper's central idea is novel and demonstrates significant improvements, there is room for enhancement. Although the study addresses environmental mismatch problems and the diffusion model at the embedding level appears novel, there exist potential risks. These include the reliance on pre-trained models for speaker recognition and potential training instability. Therefore, a score of 7 is assigned, considering that the framework is relatively basic and could benefit from expanded empirical validation, more robust ablation research, and a thorough exploration of how pre-trained model choice affects overall performance.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning Beyond Limits: Multitask Learning and Synthetic Data for Low-Resource Canonical Morpheme Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16800v1)
- **Authors**: Changbing Yang, Garrett Nicolai
- **Abstract**: We introduce a transformer-based morpheme segmentation system that augments a low-resource training signal through multitask learning and LLM-generated synthetic data. Our framework jointly predicts morphological segments and glosses from orthographic input, leveraging shared linguistic representations obtained through a common documentary process to enhance model generalization. To further address data scarcity, we integrate synthetic training data generated by large language models (LLMs) using in-context learning. Experimental results on the SIGMORPHON 2023 dataset show that our approach significantly improves word-level segmentation accuracy and morpheme-level F1-score across multiple low-resource languages.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper, and here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a transformer-based system for canonical morpheme segmentation, particularly for low-resource languages. The authors address the problem of data scarcity by employing two key strategies: (1) multitask learning, where the model jointly predicts morphological segments and glosses from orthographic input; and (2) augmenting the training data with synthetic examples generated by large language models (LLMs) using in-context learning.  The model is trained on the SIGMORPHON 2023 dataset.  The results show significant improvements in word-level segmentation accuracy and morpheme-level F1-score across various low-resource languages.  The paper demonstrates that multitask learning and synthetic data complement each other, enhancing segmentation quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The approach combines two relatively well-established techniques – multitask learning and data augmentation – within the specific context of low-resource canonical morpheme segmentation. The novelty lies primarily in the *combination* of these techniques, and *applying LLMs with in-context learning* to *generate synthetic morpheme segmentation data.* While previous work has explored multitask learning for related NLP tasks and data augmentation in general, the specific application, methodology for LLM-based synthetic data generation, and the particular task of canonical morpheme segmentation contribute to the paper's novelty. Rice et al. (2024) explored using the translation tier which requires word alignment and more data. Here, the gloss information already exists as part of the IGT framework.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its potential to improve linguistic documentation and analysis of low-resource languages. Canonical morpheme segmentation is a crucial step in this process, and the proposed method offers a way to automate and improve this task, especially when annotated data is scarce. The use of LLMs for generating synthetic data is a promising approach for addressing data limitations in many NLP tasks, and this paper provides a concrete example of its effectiveness in a challenging linguistic task. The improvement of performance is significant in terms of metrics, but the true impact is whether it saves time in language documentation and improves the process.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper addresses a relevant and important problem in NLP and linguistics.
    *   The methodology is well-motivated and clearly explained.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements over baseline methods.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component (multitask learning and synthetic data).
    *   The ethical considerations are addressed, acknowledging the potential risks associated with using LLM-generated data.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on LLMs for generating synthetic data introduces a potential for hallucination and bias. While the paper mentions this, a more detailed qualitative error analysis would be beneficial to assess the linguistic validity of the generated examples.
    *   The synthetic data generation process is limited by the existing vocabulary in the training data, restricting its ability to model truly novel linguistic forms. The model will not be able to generate new stems or morphological categories.
    *   The paper provides some specific examples of improvement, but lacks detailed examples of why one model is superior to another at a more fine-grained level.
    *   The novelty is incremental; while the *combination* is novel, the individual techniques have been explored previously.
    *   It is unknown how the LLM would generalize to truly low resource languages.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in low-resource NLP, particularly in tasks related to linguistic documentation and analysis. The use of LLMs for data augmentation and multitask learning for related linguistic tasks could be adopted and extended by other researchers. The code and data availability will also facilitate reproducibility and further exploration of the proposed methods.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field, addressing a significant problem with a well-designed and executed approach. The combination of multitask learning and LLM-generated synthetic data shows promising results, and the paper provides valuable insights into the effectiveness of these techniques. However, the incremental nature of the novelty, potential for hallucinations and biases in the synthetic data, and the reliance on potentially expensive LLM calls (not discussed in the paper) prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking work.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SOLVE: Synergy of Language-Vision and End-to-End Networks for Autonomous Driving
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16805v1)
- **Authors**: Xuesong Chen, Linjiang Huang, Tao Ma, Rongyao Fang, Shaoshuai Shi, Hongsheng Li
- **Abstract**: The integration of Vision-Language Models (VLMs) into autonomous driving systems has shown promise in addressing key challenges such as learning complexity, interpretability, and common-sense reasoning. However, existing approaches often struggle with efficient integration and realtime decision-making due to computational demands. In this paper, we introduce SOLVE, an innovative framework that synergizes VLMs with end-to-end (E2E) models to enhance autonomous vehicle planning. Our approach emphasizes knowledge sharing at the feature level through a shared visual encoder, enabling comprehensive interaction between VLM and E2E components. We propose a Trajectory Chain-of-Thought (T-CoT) paradigm, which progressively refines trajectory predictions, reducing uncertainty and improving accuracy. By employing a temporal decoupling strategy, SOLVE achieves efficient cooperation by aligning high-quality VLM outputs with E2E real-time performance. Evaluated on the nuScenes dataset, our method demonstrates significant improvements in trajectory prediction accuracy, paving the way for more robust and reliable autonomous driving systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SOLVE: Synergy of Language-Vision and End-to-End Networks for Autonomous Driving":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SOLVE, a novel framework that combines Vision-Language Models (VLMs) with end-to-end (E2E) models to improve autonomous driving systems. SOLVE emphasizes knowledge sharing at the feature level using a shared visual encoder.  A Trajectory Chain-of-Thought (T-CoT) paradigm is proposed to progressively refine trajectory predictions. The method uses a temporal decoupling strategy, aligning high-quality VLM outputs with E2E real-time performance.  Experiments on the nuScenes dataset demonstrate improvements in trajectory prediction accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the specific integration architecture and training methodology.  Combining VLMs and E2E models is not entirely new (e.g., DriveVLM), but SOLVE distinguishes itself through:
    *   **Feature-level synergy:** The shared visual encoder, coupled with the SQ-Former (Sequential Q-Former), allows for more effective knowledge transfer between the VLM and E2E components compared to solely trajectory-level cooperation as done in some previous works. The SQ-Former captures multi-aspect visual cues, potentially richer than standard visual encoders.
    *   **Trajectory Chain-of-Thought (T-CoT):** The progressive refinement of trajectories using a trajectory bank is a novel approach to trajectory prediction, allowing for a coarse-to-fine approach that leverages both pre-defined trajectories and VLM reasoning.
    *   **Temporal Decoupling:** The use of a memory-based mechanism to asynchronously enhance E2E network performance with the VLM's long-term trajectory predictions is a practical solution to address the computational cost limitations of VLMs, ensuring real-time operability.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Performance Gains:** The paper claims state-of-the-art results on the nuScenes benchmark, which, if verified, would indicate a significant advancement. The results showing improved accuracy and reduced collision rates are promising.
    *   **Addressing Limitations of E2E and VLMs:** SOLVE attempts to address key limitations of end-to-end driving systems, such as a lack of common-sense reasoning and interpretability, by integrating VLMs.  At the same time, it attempts to overcome the computational inefficiency and 3D perception challenges of directly using VLMs for planning.
    *   **Potential Impact:** If the approach generalizes well to more complex and diverse scenarios, it could have a substantial impact on the development of safer and more robust autonomous driving systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear problem statement:** The paper identifies the limitations of current autonomous driving systems and motivates the use of VLMs and E2E networks.
    *   **Well-defined architecture:** The proposed SOLVE framework is clearly described, including the SQ-Former, T-CoT, and temporal decoupling strategy.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes ablation studies to analyze the contribution of each component of SOLVE, providing insights into its design choices.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on nuScenes:** The evaluation is primarily based on the nuScenes dataset, which is a common benchmark, but has limitations in terms of diversity and complexity.  Generalization to other datasets or real-world scenarios needs to be demonstrated.
    *   **VLM Implementation Details:** The specific implementation details of the VLM (LLaVA v1.5 and its modifications) could be more thoroughly described. Justification for the choice of this particular VLM is needed.
    *   **Scalability:** While temporal decoupling helps, the computational cost of the overall system, including the VLM component, could still be a limiting factor for deployment on resource-constrained platforms.  More detailed computational performance analysis would be valuable.
    *   **Lack of Qualitative Reasoning Examples:** The paper provides qualitative results, but more concrete examples showing how the VLM's reasoning capabilities improve decision-making in challenging scenarios would strengthen the claims.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a novel and well-engineered framework for integrating VLMs and E2E models for autonomous driving. The approach addresses some key limitations of existing systems. The results on nuScenes are promising, but more comprehensive evaluation is needed to assess generalization and real-world performance.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates a clear contribution with a novel architecture and training strategy that yields significant results. The strength of combining VLMs and E2E networks is emphasized through multiple levels of integration, which sets it apart from previous works. It does have weaknesses regarding evaluation and limitations of VLMs that are not fully addressed; therefore, it isn't an extremely high-scoring paper.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Two-way Evidence self-Alignment based Dual-Gated Reasoning Enhancement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16806v1)
- **Authors**: Kexin Zhang, Junlan Chen, Daifeng Li, Yuxuan Zhang, Yangyang Feng, Bowen Deng, Weixu Chen
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) encounter difficulties in knowledge-intensive multi-step reasoning (KIMSR) tasks. One challenge is how to effectively extract and represent rationale evidence. The current methods often extract semantically relevant but logically irrelevant evidence, resulting in flawed reasoning and inaccurate responses. We propose a two-way evidence self-alignment (TW-ESA) module, which utilizes the mutual alignment between strict reasoning and LLM reasoning to enhance its understanding of the causal logic of evidence, thereby addressing the first challenge. Another challenge is how to utilize the rationale evidence and LLM's intrinsic knowledge for accurate reasoning when the evidence contains uncertainty. We propose a dual-gated reasoning enhancement (DGR) module to gradually fuse useful knowledge of LLM within strict reasoning, which can enable the model to perform accurate reasoning by focusing on causal elements in the evidence and exhibit greater robustness. The two modules are collaboratively trained in a unified framework ESA-DGR. Extensive experiments on three diverse and challenging KIMSR datasets reveal that ESA-DGR significantly surpasses state-of-the-art LLM-based fine-tuning methods, with remarkable average improvements of 4% in exact match (EM) and 5% in F1 score. The implementation code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/ESA-DGR-2BF8.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ESA-DGR, a framework designed to enhance knowledge-intensive multi-step reasoning (KIMSR) in large language models (LLMs). ESA-DGR addresses two key challenges: (1) effectively extracting and representing rationale evidence, and (2) accurately reasoning using both rationale evidence and LLM's intrinsic knowledge, especially when the evidence is uncertain.  The framework comprises two main modules:

1.  **Two-way Evidence Self-Alignment (TW-ESA):** Aims to extract rationale evidence from retrieved evidence by aligning strict reasoning with LLM reasoning. This enhances the model's understanding of causal logic.

2.  **Dual-Gated Reasoning Enhancement (DGR):** Utilizes a dual-gating mechanism to gradually fuse LLM's internal knowledge with strict reasoning, improving accuracy and robustness, especially when evidence contains uncertainty.

The two modules are trained collaboratively within a unified framework. Experiments on three KIMSR datasets demonstrate that ESA-DGR outperforms existing LLM-based fine-tuning methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses Relevant Problem:** The paper targets a known weakness of LLMs in KIMSR tasks, specifically the difficulties in extracting rationale evidence and handling uncertainty in reasoning.
    *   **Novel Modules:** The TW-ESA and DGR modules propose a novel approach to improve LLM performance in KIMSR by using mutual alignment and gated reasoning.
    *   **Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on multiple challenging KIMSR datasets. Ablation studies provide insight into the contribution of each module. The analysis of query efficiency shows that their approach improves efficiency.
    *   **Interpretability:** Visualizations show improved disentanglement of textual input into rationale and non-rationale representations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Heuristics:** The approach depends on external knowledge sources (BM25 via Elasticsearch). The reliance on GPT-4 ratings for evaluating usefulness may introduce bias.
    *   **Complexity:** The framework involves several components (RIE, TW-ESA, DGR, GRPO), increasing complexity. While ablation studies attempt to isolate the contributions of each component, interactions between components may not be fully captured.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:** Some individual components may adapt existing techniques. The core novelty lies in the combination and interaction of these components within the ESA-DGR framework.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** The paper provides some intuition but lacks deep theoretical analysis. A theoretical grounding of the dual-gating mechanism to enhance reasoning capabilities may strengthen the paper.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The main novelty comes from the two novel modules ESA and DGR, the novel way that strict reasoning and LLM reasoning can be integrated, and the performance gains shown in this paper.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of KIMSR by proposing a novel and effective framework for improving LLM reasoning with external knowledge. The empirical results and ablation studies support the effectiveness of the proposed approach. However, the reliance on heuristic components and the lack of theoretical grounding limit the paper's overall impact.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper is a valuable contribution that introduces a compelling and effective method for addressing core challenges in KIMSR. The empirical results are strong, and the proposed modules have intuitive explanations. However, some limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score. While providing experimental evidence, the authors could have offered more theoretical support for the efficacy of the proposed alignment and gated mechanisms. Also, the reliance on existing techniques (BM25 and GPT-4 evaluation) and the relatively high complexity are notable drawbacks.

Therefore, the paper achieves a score of 7.5, placing it as a strong contribution with substantial empirical validation and promising future directions.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10
