### DeepRec: Towards a Deep Dive Into the Item Space with Large Language Model Based Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16810v1)
- **Authors**: Bowen Zheng, Xiaolei Wang, Enze Liu, Xi Wang, Lu Hongyu, Yu Chen, Wayne Xin Zhao, Ji-Rong Wen
- **Abstract**: Recently, large language models (LLMs) have been introduced into recommender systems (RSs), either to enhance traditional recommendation models (TRMs) or serve as recommendation backbones. However, existing LLM-based RSs often do not fully exploit the complementary advantages of LLMs (e.g., world knowledge and reasoning) and TRMs (e.g., recommendation-specific knowledge and efficiency) to fully explore the item space. To address this, we propose DeepRec, a novel LLM-based RS that enables autonomous multi-turn interactions between LLMs and TRMs for deep exploration of the item space. In each interaction turn, LLMs reason over user preferences and interact with TRMs to retrieve candidate items. After multi-turn interactions, LLMs rank the retrieved items to generate the final recommendations. We adopt reinforcement learning(RL) based optimization and propose novel designs from three aspects: recommendation model based data rollout, recommendation-oriented hierarchical rewards, and a two-stage RL training strategy. For data rollout, we introduce a preference-aware TRM, with which LLMs interact to construct trajectory data. For rewards, we design a hierarchical reward function that involves both process-level and outcome-level rewards to optimize the interaction process and recommendation performance, respectively. For RL training, we develop a two-stage training strategy, where the first stage aims to guide LLMs to interact with TRMs and the second stage focuses on performance improvement. Experiments on public datasets demonstrate that DeepRec significantly outperforms both traditional and LLM-based baselines, offering a new paradigm for deep exploration in recommendation systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepRec, a novel recommender system leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) to deeply explore the item space through multi-turn interactions with traditional recommendation models (TRMs).  DeepRec facilitates autonomous reasoning-retrieval between LLMs and TRMs. In each turn, the LLM analyzes user preferences and uses TRMs to retrieve candidate items.  A reinforcement learning (RL) framework is adopted with novel designs for data rollout (using a preference-aware TRM), hierarchical rewards (process and outcome levels), and a two-stage RL training strategy. Experimental results on public datasets demonstrate that DeepRec outperforms both traditional and LLM-based baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using multi-turn reasoning-retrieval between LLMs and TRMs is reasonably novel in the recommendation context. Existing LLM-based RS approaches primarily focus on either enhancing TRMs or using LLMs as standalone recommenders. DeepRec's iterative interaction approach has the potential to better explore the item space and refine recommendations. The RL framework with the specific reward designs and the two-stage training adds to the novelty, though RL has been used in LLM contexts before.

*   **Significance:** The work's significance stems from addressing the limitations of existing LLM-based RS that don't fully exploit the complementary strengths of LLMs (knowledge and reasoning) and TRMs (recommendation-specific knowledge and efficiency). DeepRec's approach of combining these models via autonomous multi-turn interaction addresses this gap. The improvement over baselines demonstrates the practical benefit of the approach. The proposed RL-based optimization can be a useful tool for future research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-motivated problem and clear explanation of the limitations of existing LLM-based RS.
    *   The multi-turn reasoning-retrieval approach is a sound idea for deeper item space exploration.
    *   The RL framework with the novel designs for data rollout, hierarchical rewards, and training strategy contribute significantly to the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   The use of preference-aware TRM is a smart decision.
    *   The empirical results show a significant improvement over baselines, supporting the effectiveness of DeepRec.
    *   Careful construction of system prompt design for LLMs which facilitates interaction with TRMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The computational cost associated with multi-turn interaction and RL training might be a concern for large-scale real-world deployments, but the paper does not deeply explore the efficiency aspect.
    *   The impact of specific components of the RL framework could be further analyzed with more granular ablation studies. For example, exploring different preference diversity metrics or the weight decay strategies.
    *   The paper primarily focuses on enhancing recommendation *performance* and does not address issues like fairness, diversity (besides preference diversity reward) in the final recommendations, or user experience.

*   **Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. DeepRec represents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM-based recommender systems. Its novelty lies in the multi-turn reasoning-retrieval interaction and the RL framework tailored for this interaction. The experimental results clearly show the effectiveness of DeepRec. However, the lack of a thorough efficiency analysis and deeper exploration of aspects beyond performance, such as fairness and diversity, prevents a higher score. Further research is needed to address these limitations and solidify the practical applicability of DeepRec in real-world scenarios. Its focus on improving recommendation outcomes coupled with a design that integrates LLMs with other recommendation methods makes this paper significant.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### KTAE: A Model-Free Algorithm to Key-Tokens Advantage Estimation in Mathematical Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16826v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Sun, Wen Yang, Pu Jian, Qianlong Du, Fuwei Cui, Shuo Ren, Jiajun Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances have demonstrated that integrating reinforcement learning with rule-based rewards can significantly enhance the reasoning capabilities of large language models, even without supervised fine-tuning. However, prevalent reinforcement learning algorithms such as GRPO and its variants like DAPO, suffer from a coarse granularity issue when computing the advantage. Specifically, they compute rollout-level advantages that assign identical values to every token within a sequence, failing to capture token-specific contributions and hindering effective learning. To address this limitation, we propose Key-token Advantage Estimation (KTAE) - a novel algorithm that estimates fine-grained, token-level advantages without introducing additional models. KTAE leverages the correctness of sampled rollouts and applies statistical analysis to quantify the importance of individual tokens within a sequence to the final outcome. This quantified token-level importance is then combined with the rollout-level advantage to obtain a more fine-grained token-level advantage estimation. Empirical results show that models trained with GRPO+KTAE and DAPO+KTAE outperform baseline methods across five mathematical reasoning benchmarks. Notably, they achieve higher accuracy with shorter responses and even surpass R1-Distill-Qwen-1.5B using the same base model.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Key-token Advantage Estimation (KTAE), a model-free reinforcement learning algorithm designed to enhance the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in mathematical problem-solving. KTAE addresses the coarse granularity issue in existing RL methods like GRPO and DAPO, which assign identical advantages to all tokens within a sequence.  KTAE leverages the correctness of sampled rollouts and applies statistical analysis (Fisher's exact test and Information Gain) to quantify the importance of individual tokens to the final outcome, thus providing a finer-grained token-level advantage estimation.  The paper demonstrates that integrating KTAE with GRPO or DAPO improves performance on several mathematical reasoning benchmarks, resulting in higher accuracy and shorter response lengths.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of KTAE – using statistical analysis on token occurrences within successful/unsuccessful rollouts to refine advantage estimation – is novel within the context of RL fine-tuning for LLMs.  While statistical association measures (like Fisher's exact test and information gain) are well-established, their application to weighting token contributions in RL for LLMs is a significant contribution. The model-free aspect, meaning it doesn't require training additional reward models, is also noteworthy.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a crucial limitation of existing RL methods (coarse granularity of advantage estimation) in the context of mathematical reasoning, which is considered a strong benchmark for AI capabilities. The reported empirical gains (improved accuracy and shorter response lengths) are substantial, indicating a practical benefit. The plug-and-play nature of KTAE makes it easily adaptable to existing RL frameworks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation. The paper clearly identifies the limitations of rollout-level advantage estimation and provides a compelling argument for token-level refinement.
    *   Well-defined algorithm. KTAE is presented in a structured and understandable manner.
    *   Strong empirical validation.  The paper demonstrates improved performance across multiple benchmarks using different base models (1.5B and 7B), supporting the effectiveness of the algorithm. Ablation studies are performed to understand the contribution of different modules in the algorithms.
    *   Model-free nature. Which is an important aspect for LLMs fine-tuning.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited Scale Validation: The experiments primarily focus on 1.5B and 7B parameter models. The effectiveness of KTAE on significantly larger (e.g., 100B+) models isn't extensively explored.
    *   Domain Specificity: The evaluation is limited to mathematical reasoning. While the core concept is potentially generalizable, the paper lacks validation in other reasoning domains or NLP tasks. This limits the broader applicability claims.
    *   Complexity: The combination of statistical tests and token frequency calculations in the key-token value is somewhat complex. A simpler, more streamlined approach might be desirable.
    *   Parameter Tuning:  KTAE introduces several hyperparameters (h1, h2, h3, k1, b).  The paper lacks a detailed analysis of how these parameters influence performance. Understanding the sensitivity to these parameters is crucial for practical application.
    *   The paper states that KTAE can theoretically be applied to any task beyond rule-based rewards, but no analysis or validation is provided on this claim.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in RL fine-tuning for LLMs, particularly in tasks requiring detailed reasoning. The idea of using statistical analysis to understand token importance can inspire new approaches to more efficient and targeted learning.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel and practically useful approach to improving the reasoning capabilities of LLMs through reinforcement learning.  The improvements are well-supported by empirical evidence, and the model-free nature of KTAE is a valuable feature.  However, the limitations regarding scale validation, domain specificity, and hyperparameter sensitivity prevent a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unlearning Isn't Deletion: Investigating Reversibility of Machine Unlearning in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16831v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyu Xu, Xiang Yue, Yang Liu, Qingqing Ye, Haibo Hu, Minxin Du
- **Abstract**: Unlearning in large language models (LLMs) is intended to remove the influence of specific data, yet current evaluations rely heavily on token-level metrics such as accuracy and perplexity. We show that these metrics can be misleading: models often appear to forget, but their original behavior can be rapidly restored with minimal fine-tuning, revealing that unlearning may obscure information rather than erase it. To diagnose this phenomenon, we introduce a representation-level evaluation framework using PCA-based similarity and shift, centered kernel alignment, and Fisher information. Applying this toolkit across six unlearning methods, three domains (text, code, math), and two open-source LLMs, we uncover a critical distinction between reversible and irreversible forgetting. In reversible cases, models suffer token-level collapse yet retain latent features; in irreversible cases, deeper representational damage occurs. We further provide a theoretical account linking shallow weight perturbations near output layers to misleading unlearning signals, and show that reversibility is modulated by task type and hyperparameters. Our findings reveal a fundamental gap in current evaluation practices and establish a new diagnostic foundation for trustworthy unlearning in LLMs. We provide a unified toolkit for analyzing LLM representation changes under unlearning and relearning: https://github.com/XiaoyuXU1/Representational_Analysis_Tools.git.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Unlearning Isn't Deletion: Investigating Reversibility of Machine Unlearning in LLMs":

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the limitations of current machine unlearning techniques in Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors demonstrate that relying solely on token-level metrics like accuracy and perplexity can be misleading, as models often appear to forget specific data but can rapidly relearn it with minimal fine-tuning. They introduce a representation-level evaluation framework using PCA-based similarity and shift, centered kernel alignment (CKA), and Fisher information to diagnose the reversibility of unlearning. Through extensive experiments across various unlearning methods, domains, and LLMs, they identify a critical distinction between reversible and irreversible forgetting, showing that token-level collapse in reversible cases doesn't necessarily imply true erasure of latent features. Finally, they provide a theoretical analysis connecting shallow weight perturbations to misleading unlearning signals and modulate the reversibility by task type and hyperparameter settings. They provide a toolkit to analyze LLM representation changes under unlearning and relearning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes a significant contribution by highlighting a fundamental flaw in how LLM unlearning is currently evaluated. The insight that token-level metrics can be deceptive and that models may simply *suppress* rather than *erase* information is crucial. The representation-level evaluation framework is a valuable tool for more thorough analysis. The concept of reversible vs. irreversible forgetting, even when superficially the token metrics don't distinguish the cases, is insightful. However, the specific representation analysis techniques (PCA, CKA, FIM) have been used in other contexts to study model internals, so the novelty is in *applying* and *combining* these within the unlearning domain.

*   **Significance and Impact:** The paper addresses a critical gap in the field of machine unlearning. Reliable unlearning is essential for privacy, safety, and compliance with regulations like the "right to be forgotten." Demonstrating that existing methods can be easily circumvented raises serious concerns about the trustworthiness of current unlearning techniques. The paper's findings directly impact how future unlearning methods are developed and evaluated. It also provides a necessary cautionary tale about the limitations of readily accessible metrics and reinforces the need for a deeper understanding of model internals during unlearning. The toolkit makes a great contribution to further this direction.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper effectively frames the problem of misleading token-level metrics in unlearning.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experimental design is well-structured, encompassing multiple unlearning methods, datasets, and LLMs.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The identification and characterization of reversible vs. irreversible forgetting are well-supported by empirical evidence and theoretical analysis.
    *   **Practical Contribution:** The representation-level evaluation framework provides a valuable tool for future research.
    *   **Solid Theory:** Connecting the reversibility to the extent of weight perturbation provides some theoretical grounding.
    *   **The inclusion of the toolkit** makes it easy for others to build upon their work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Scalability of Representation Analysis:** While the representation-level evaluation is valuable, it can be computationally expensive and challenging to apply to extremely large models. The paper could have addressed how to improve scalability.
    *   **Limited scope of LLMs**: While the authors have made an effort to utilize different models, they all have very similar architectures and similar pre-training data.
    *   **Limited solutions proposed**: While the paper strongly highlights the problem with existing LLM unlearning methods, its main strength lies in exposing these problems and making relevant research available for others to solve.
    *   **The weight analysis is not comprehensive.** The weight analysis provides a helpful theoretical explanation of how widespread vs. localized parameter changes relate to (ir)reversible forgetting, but the analysis could be further strengthened by including experiments that directly manipulate weight changes during unlearning.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence the development of more robust and trustworthy LLM unlearning algorithms. It also sets a higher standard for evaluating the effectiveness of unlearning methods, encouraging researchers to move beyond superficial token-level metrics. Finally, it opens up new avenues for research into understanding and controlling the internal representations of LLMs during unlearning.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper identifies a critical weakness in current LLM unlearning practices and provides a novel and useful diagnostic framework. While the individual techniques within the framework are not entirely new, their application and combination within the unlearning context represent a significant advancement. The theoretical analysis strengthens the findings, and the comprehensive evaluation provides strong empirical support. The main drawbacks are some remaining scalability concerns with the analysis framework, the weight change manipulation, and the lack of a fully-realized solution to the identified problem. However, the paper's ability to shift the field towards more rigorous and reliable unlearning evaluation justifies a strong score of 8. The work could be improved with more focus on scalability and a stronger, direct connection between weight analysis and unlearning approaches, and, furthermore, a practical unlearning mechanism would make the paper even more valuable.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### From EduVisBench to EduVisAgent: A Benchmark and Multi-Agent Framework for Pedagogical Visualization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16832v1)
- **Authors**: Haonian Ji, Shi Qiu, Siyang Xin, Siwei Han, Zhaorun Chen, Hongyi Wang, Dake Zhang, Huaxiu Yao
- **Abstract**: While foundation models (FMs), such as diffusion models and large vision-language models (LVLMs), have been widely applied in educational contexts, their ability to generate pedagogically effective visual explanations remains limited. Most existing approaches focus primarily on textual reasoning, overlooking the critical role of structured and interpretable visualizations in supporting conceptual understanding. To better assess the visual reasoning capabilities of FMs in educational settings, we introduce EduVisBench, a multi-domain, multi-level benchmark. EduVisBench features diverse STEM problem sets requiring visually grounded solutions, along with a fine-grained evaluation rubric informed by pedagogical theory. Our empirical analysis reveals that existing models frequently struggle with the inherent challenge of decomposing complex reasoning and translating it into visual representations aligned with human cognitive processes. To address these limitations, we propose EduVisAgent, a multi-agent collaborative framework that coordinates specialized agents for instructional planning, reasoning decomposition, metacognitive prompting, and visualization design. Experimental results show that EduVisAgent substantially outperforms all baselines, achieving a 40.2% improvement and delivering more educationally aligned visualizations. EduVisBench and EduVisAgent are available at https://github.com/aiming-lab/EduVisBench and https://github.com/aiming-lab/EduVisAgent.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EduVisBench, a multi-domain, multi-level benchmark for evaluating the visual reasoning capabilities of foundation models (FMs) in educational contexts. It highlights the limitations of current FMs in generating pedagogically effective visual explanations, particularly their difficulty in decomposing complex reasoning into visually representable steps. To address these limitations, the paper proposes EduVisAgent, a multi-agent collaborative framework that coordinates specialized agents for instructional planning, reasoning decomposition, metacognitive prompting, and visualization design. Experimental results demonstrate that EduVisAgent significantly outperforms baseline models in generating educationally aligned visualizations. The paper makes EduVisBench and EduVisAgent publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addressing a Gap:** The paper tackles a significant gap in the application of FMs in education – the generation of effective visual aids. Existing work primarily focuses on textual explanations, neglecting the crucial role of visualization.
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** EduVisBench appears to be a well-designed benchmark with a multi-domain, multi-level structure and a fine-grained evaluation rubric. The focus on pedagogical criteria such as contextual relevance, visual clarity, and interactive engagement is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Multi-Agent Approach:** The EduVisAgent framework leverages the strengths of different specialized agents to create more coherent and pedagogically sound visualizations. The modularity and pedagogical principles built into the agent design are innovative.
    *   **Significant Performance Improvement:** The experimental results demonstrate a substantial performance improvement with EduVisAgent compared to baseline models, showcasing the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Public Availability:** Making the benchmark and framework publicly available promotes further research and development in this area.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalizability Claim:** The benchmark, though diverse, is limited to three STEM subjects and a set of subdomains. Claiming broad generalizability to all education domains would be an overstatement.
    *   **Reliance on GPT-4 for Evaluation:** While GPT-4 is advanced, it's still a model. The reliance on it for evaluating pedagogical effectiveness raises concerns. Human evaluation, though costly, would strengthen the validity of the benchmark.
    *   **Limited Baseline Variety:** The choice of baselines could be expanded. Including more specialized visualization tools besides v0 might provide a more nuanced comparison.
    *   **Reproducibility Concerns:** Details about the agent prompting strategy and system implementation are needed to ensure reproducibility of results.
    *   **Scalability and Cost Considerations:** The multi-agent architecture may come with computational overhead. Exploring the scalability and cost-effectiveness of EduVisAgent for large-scale educational applications would be valuable.
    *   **No Human Subject Testing** There is no mention of performing the actual usability testing with human subjects

*   **Novelty:** The combination of a multi-domain benchmark with a multi-agent framework specifically designed for pedagogical visualization is relatively novel. While existing research has explored FMs in education, the focus on generating high-quality, educationally aligned visuals through a modular agent-based approach represents a distinct contribution. The fine-grained evaluation rubric adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the application of FMs in education. By addressing the limitations of existing models in generating effective visual aids, the paper paves the way for more engaging and impactful learning experiences. The benchmark can serve as a valuable tool for evaluating and comparing different approaches to visual pedagogical reasoning.

**Justification for the Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, the paper represents a solid and valuable contribution to the field. The novelty lies in the specific combination of a purpose-built benchmark and a multi-agent framework designed with pedagogical principles in mind. The EduVisAgent system is well motivated and demonstrates clear performance gains. The significance stems from addressing a critical gap in the application of FMs in education, potentially leading to more effective and engaging learning experiences.

However, the reliance on GPT-4 for evaluation, the need for more comprehensive human testing and more detail on implementation, and the potential generalizability concerns prevent the paper from achieving a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### SimpleDeepSearcher: Deep Information Seeking via Web-Powered Reasoning Trajectory Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16834v1)
- **Authors**: Shuang Sun, Huatong Song, Yuhao Wang, Ruiyang Ren, Jinhao Jiang, Junjie Zhang, Fei Bai, Jia Deng, Wayne Xin Zhao, Zheng Liu, Lei Fang, Zhongyuan Wang, Ji-Rong Wen
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems have advanced large language models (LLMs) in complex deep search scenarios requiring multi-step reasoning and iterative information retrieval. However, existing approaches face critical limitations that lack high-quality training trajectories or suffer from the distributional mismatches in simulated environments and prohibitive computational costs for real-world deployment. This paper introduces SimpleDeepSearcher, a lightweight yet effective framework that bridges this gap through strategic data engineering rather than complex training paradigms. Our approach synthesizes high-quality training data by simulating realistic user interactions in live web search environments, coupled with a multi-criteria curation strategy that optimizes the diversity and quality of input and output side. Experiments on five benchmarks across diverse domains demonstrate that SFT on only 871 curated samples yields significant improvements over RL-based baselines. Our work establishes SFT as a viable pathway by systematically addressing the data-scarce bottleneck, offering practical insights for efficient deep search systems. Our code is available at https://github.com/RUCAIBox/SimpleDeepSearcher.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SimpleDeepSearcher: Deep Information Seeking via Web-Powered Reasoning Trajectory Synthesis":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SimpleDeepSearcher, a framework designed to improve deep search capabilities in large language models (LLMs). It addresses the limitations of existing retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems that struggle with high-quality training trajectories or face distributional mismatches and high computational costs.  SimpleDeepSearcher focuses on strategic data engineering by synthesizing high-quality training data. This is achieved through a three-fold process: (1) simulating realistic user interactions in live web search environments, (2) employing a diversity-aware query sampling strategy, and (3) using a multi-criteria curation strategy to optimize input and output data quality. The paper demonstrates that supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on a relatively small curated dataset (871 samples) significantly outperforms reinforcement learning (RL)-based baselines on several benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in its strategic data engineering approach to deep search. While the individual components like RAG and SFT are not new, the combination and emphasis on carefully constructing a high-quality, small-scale training dataset from real web environments is a significant contribution. The idea of prioritizing data quality over quantity in the context of deep search with LLMs is a valuable insight. This contrasts with many other approaches that rely on either large-scale data or complex RL training paradigms.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant for several reasons. Firstly, it demonstrates that SFT can be a viable and efficient alternative to RL for training deep search systems, addressing the computational cost and distributional mismatch challenges often associated with RL. Secondly, it provides a practical framework for constructing high-quality training data for deep search, including detailed methods for query sampling and response curation. Thirdly, the experimental results across various benchmarks suggest that the proposed approach is effective and generalizable. The ablation studies also reveal valuable insights regarding the contribution of each component.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing approaches to deep search.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The data synthesis and curation pipeline is well-defined and explained.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper presents compelling experimental results comparing SimpleDeepSearcher to strong baselines.
    *   **Detailed Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of different components.
    *   **Code Availability:** Code availability enhances reproducibility and encourages future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Commercial APIs:** The reliance on commercial search APIs could limit the reproducibility and accessibility of the research for others.
    *   **Scale of Evaluation:** While the paper demonstrates the effectiveness of the method, a more extensive evaluation with larger datasets might strengthen the conclusions.
    *   **Model Size Limitation:** The distillation training was conducted on 7B and 32B models due to resource limitations. Evaluation on larger models would further substantiate the claims.
    *   **Complexity of Multi-hop Questions:** The paper mentions that the multi-hop questions used for distillation were relatively simple. Synthesizing more complex multi-hop questions could improve the model's capabilities further.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging researchers to focus on data quality and efficient training techniques for deep search. The SimpleDeepSearcher framework can serve as a valuable resource for developing and evaluating future deep search systems. The finding that small, carefully curated datasets can achieve significant performance gains challenges the assumption that large-scale training is always necessary.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, and the novelty and significance of the work, I assign a score of **8**. The paper presents a novel and effective approach to deep search with LLMs, addressing critical limitations of existing methods. The focus on strategic data engineering and the demonstration of SFT as a viable alternative to RL are valuable contributions. While some weaknesses exist, such as reliance on commercial APIs and limitations in the scale of evaluation, the overall impact of the work is significant. The paper's potential to influence future research and development in deep search warrants a high score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Fact-R1: Towards Explainable Video Misinformation Detection with Deep Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16836v1)
- **Authors**: Fanrui Zhang, Dian Li, Qiang Zhang, Chenjun, sinbadliu, Junxiong Lin, Jiahong Yan, Jiawei Liu, Zheng-Jun Zha
- **Abstract**: The rapid spread of multimodal misinformation on social media has raised growing concerns, while research on video misinformation detection remains limited due to the lack of large-scale, diverse datasets. Existing methods often overfit to rigid templates and lack deep reasoning over deceptive content. To address these challenges, we introduce FakeVV, a large-scale benchmark comprising over 100,000 video-text pairs with fine-grained, interpretable annotations. In addition, we further propose Fact-R1, a novel framework that integrates deep reasoning with collaborative rule-based reinforcement learning. Fact-R1 is trained through a three-stage process: (1) misinformation long-Chain-of-Thought (CoT) instruction tuning, (2) preference alignment via Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), and (3) Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) using a novel verifiable reward function. This enables Fact-R1 to exhibit emergent reasoning behaviors comparable to those observed in advanced text-based reinforcement learning systems, but in the more complex multimodal misinformation setting. Our work establishes a new paradigm for misinformation detection, bridging large-scale video understanding, reasoning-guided alignment, and interpretable verification.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FACT-R1: Towards Explainable Video Misinformation Detection with Deep Reasoning":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenging problem of video misinformation detection, which is exacerbated by the lack of large-scale, diverse datasets and the limitations of existing methods in performing deep reasoning over deceptive content. The authors introduce two key contributions:

1.  **FakeVV:**  A new large-scale benchmark dataset comprising over 100,000 video-text pairs with fine-grained, interpretable annotations of misinformation. This dataset aims to address the limitations of existing datasets in terms of scale, diversity, and annotation quality.
2.  **Fact-R1:** A novel framework that integrates deep reasoning with collaborative rule-based reinforcement learning for misinformation detection. The framework is trained in three stages: a) misinformation long-Chain-of-Thought (CoT) instruction tuning, b) preference alignment via Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), and c) Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) using a novel verifiable reward function.  Fact-R1 is designed to exhibit emergent reasoning behaviors akin to advanced text-based reinforcement learning systems.

The authors present experimental results demonstrating that Fact-R1 achieves state-of-the-art performance on the new FakeVV dataset and existing benchmark datasets. They also provide ablation studies and explainability analyses to demonstrate the contributions of the different components of Fact-R1.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**
    *   **Dataset:** The FakeVV dataset is a significant contribution. The scale and the fine-grained, interpretable annotations significantly advance the field. The emphasis on out-of-context misinformation and the non-random entity replacement strategy for generating challenging samples represent a clear step forward compared to previous datasets.
    *   **Framework:** The Fact-R1 framework demonstrates novelty in its architecture and training methodology. Combining deep reasoning with rule-based reinforcement learning for misinformation detection is a novel approach. The specific three-stage training process (CoT instruction tuning, DPO, GRPO) and the design of the task-specific verifiable reward function is innovative.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The FakeVV dataset addresses a critical bottleneck in the field by providing a resource for training and evaluating more robust and generalizable misinformation detection models.
    *   Fact-R1 provides a new paradigm for misinformation detection, moving beyond simple pattern recognition to more complex, explainable reasoning. This is crucial for building trust and transparency in automated systems. The explainability analysis provided in the paper, though limited, helps understand the decision-making process of the model.
    *   The approach has the potential to be extended to other domains where reasoning and explainability are important.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The scale and diversity of the FakeVV dataset represent a significant leap forward for the field.
    *   The Fact-R1 framework achieves state-of-the-art performance.
    *   The explainability analysis, while preliminary, provides valuable insights into the model's reasoning process.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly presents the problem, approach, and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The explainability analysis, while a good start, relies on a judge model (GPT-4o-mini) and is inherently limited by the capabilities of that model. A more comprehensive evaluation of explainability involving human evaluation or more direct measures of reasoning quality would strengthen the paper.
    *   The reliance on commercial LLMs like GPT-4o for parts of the pipeline (e.g., caption generation, fake sample generation) makes the pipeline less accessible and harder to reproduce for researchers with limited resources.
    *   The discussion of potential negative societal impacts is somewhat brief. A more thorough discussion of the ethical considerations and responsible use of the technology would be valuable. The work could have highlighted a more detailed account of the measures taken to ensure responsible data handling to mitigate potential risks.
    *   The model architecture used for Fact-R1 is based on Qwen2.5-VL, an existing LLM. The specific modifications and fine-tuning strategies for misinformation detection need to be emphasized to better convey the novelty of the proposed framework.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The FakeVV dataset is likely to become a widely used benchmark in the field, driving further research and development of video misinformation detection models.
    *   The Fact-R1 framework provides a new direction for research, inspiring further work on deep reasoning and rule-based reinforcement learning for misinformation detection.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a strong contribution to the field of video misinformation detection. The FakeVV dataset fills a critical gap, and the Fact-R1 framework demonstrates a novel and effective approach to the problem. The state-of-the-art results and initial explainability analysis are promising. However, the limitations in the explainability analysis, reliance on commercial LLMs, and lack of a more thorough ethical discussion prevent a higher score. The potential for the FakeVV dataset to drive future research justifies a relatively high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### R1-Compress: Long Chain-of-Thought Compression via Chunk Compression and Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16838v1)
- **Authors**: Yibo Wang, Li Shen, Huanjin Yao, Tiansheng Huang, Rui Liu, Naiqiang Tan, Jiaxing Huang, Kai Zhang, Dacheng Tao
- **Abstract**: Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning enhances large language models (LLMs) by enabling step-by-step problem-solving, yet its extension to Long-CoT introduces substantial computational overhead due to increased token length. Existing compression approaches -- instance-level and token-level -- either sacrifice essential local reasoning signals like reflection or yield incoherent outputs. To address these limitations, we propose R1-Compress, a two-stage chunk-level compression framework that preserves both local information and coherence. Our method segments Long-CoT into manageable chunks, applies LLM-driven inner-chunk compression, and employs an inter-chunk search mechanism to select the short and coherent sequence. Experiments on Qwen2.5-Instruct models across MATH500, AIME24, and GPQA-Diamond demonstrate that R1-Compress significantly reduces token usage while maintaining comparable reasoning accuracy. On MATH500, R1-Compress achieves an accuracy of 92.4%, with only a 0.6% drop compared to the Long-CoT baseline, while reducing token usage by about 20%. Source code will be available at https://github.com/w-yibo/R1-Compress
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R1-Compress, a novel two-stage chunk-level compression framework designed to reduce the token length of Long Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning while preserving local information and coherence. R1-Compress first segments Long-CoT into manageable chunks, applies LLM-driven inner-chunk compression, and then employs an inter-chunk search mechanism to select a short and coherent sequence. The key idea is to address limitations of existing instance-level and token-level compression approaches that either sacrifice essential reasoning signals (like reflection) or produce incoherent outputs. Experimental results on the MATH500, AIME24, and GPQA-Diamond datasets using Qwen2.5-Instruct models demonstrate that R1-Compress significantly reduces token usage while maintaining comparable reasoning accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in the chunk-level compression approach combined with an inter-chunk search mechanism. This tackles a crucial problem in Long-CoT, namely the computational overhead, while maintaining reasoning accuracy. The combination of inner-chunk compression (using prompting) and inter-chunk selection via a search model is a unique contribution. It effectively addresses the trade-off between compression and coherence, which is a common problem in existing compression methods.
*   **Significance:** Long-CoT models are known for their resource intensiveness, thus compressing them has significant practical implications for deployment and scalability. The paper's focus on preserving reasoning accuracy during compression is essential, making LLMs that use CoT more usable and accessible.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The proposed R1-Compress tackles a relevant problem with practical significance.
    *   The chunk-level compression coupled with inter-chunk search addresses the limitations of both instance-level and token-level compression effectively.
    *   The paper contains extensive empirical evaluations, with experiments on popular benchmarks and models, as well as thorough ablation studies, justifying the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The analysis on reflection preservation and coherence is also well done.
    *   The code is available, enhancing the reproducibility and impact.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on LLMs for inner-chunk compression means the performance is still dependent on the LLM's abilities and prompt engineering. The chunking mechanism and length filtering can be seen as a heuristic approach, however, the search mechanism mitigates this issue.
    *   Although the experimental section is thorough, most benchmarks used focus on math problems. There is a lack of diverse tasks and more extensive evaluation on tasks like logical reasoning and code generation.
    *   While the approach reduces tokens, it introduces additional computation from both the inner-chunk compression and inter-chunk search. A clear comparison of overall computational efficiency (e.g., wall-clock time) compared to baseline methods would be beneficial. This is an important factor for real-world deployment.
    *   The paper describes the use of DeepSeek R1 and Open Math R1 in their methodology. DeepSeek R1 is not released to the general public, thus potentially making the method hard to reproduce for some users.
    *   The results presented by C3OT (77.4 accuracy on MATH500) seem to be drastically different to the ones published by the original authors (over 90%). This may indicate issues with the evaluation pipeline.

*   **Potential Impact:** If widely adopted, R1-Compress could lead to more efficient and scalable deployment of Long-CoT models. The framework's modularity could also encourage further research into improved chunk compression and inter-chunk coherence strategies.
*   **Score Justification:** The paper presents a novel and practical solution to a critical challenge in the Long-CoT research area. The combined chunking and search strategy is innovative, and the empirical results demonstrate the method's effectiveness in reducing token usage while preserving reasoning accuracy. Although there are weaknesses relating to benchmark diversity, additional computational overhead analysis, and external dependencies, the strengths and overall impact of the work justify a score of 7. The approach improves both LLMs resource efficiency and reasoning quality, showing its great significance.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LaViDa: A Large Diffusion Language Model for Multimodal Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16839v1)
- **Authors**: Shufan Li, Konstantinos Kallidromitis, Hritik Bansal, Akash Gokul, Yusuke Kato, Kazuki Kozuka, Jason Kuen, Zhe Lin, Kai-Wei Chang, Aditya Grover
- **Abstract**: Modern Vision-Language Models (VLMs) can solve a wide range of tasks requiring visual reasoning. In real-world scenarios, desirable properties for VLMs include fast inference and controllable generation (e.g., constraining outputs to adhere to a desired format). However, existing autoregressive (AR) VLMs like LLaVA struggle in these aspects. Discrete diffusion models (DMs) offer a promising alternative, enabling parallel decoding for faster inference and bidirectional context for controllable generation through text-infilling. While effective in language-only settings, DMs' potential for multimodal tasks is underexplored. We introduce LaViDa, a family of VLMs built on DMs. We build LaViDa by equipping DMs with a vision encoder and jointly fine-tune the combined parts for multimodal instruction following. To address challenges encountered, LaViDa incorporates novel techniques such as complementary masking for effective training, prefix KV cache for efficient inference, and timestep shifting for high-quality sampling. Experiments show that LaViDa achieves competitive or superior performance to AR VLMs on multi-modal benchmarks such as MMMU, while offering unique advantages of DMs, including flexible speed-quality tradeoff, controllability, and bidirectional reasoning. On COCO captioning, LaViDa surpasses Open-LLaVa-Next-8B by +4.1 CIDEr with 1.92x speedup. On bidirectional tasks, it achieves +59% improvement on Constrained Poem Completion. These results demonstrate LaViDa as a strong alternative to AR VLMs. Code and models will be released in the camera-ready version.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LaViDa, a novel family of Vision-Language Models (VLMs) built on discrete diffusion models (DMs).  Unlike prevalent autoregressive (AR) VLMs, LaViDa leverages the benefits of DMs, such as parallel decoding for faster inference and bidirectional context modeling for controllable generation. The authors address the challenges in adapting DMs to multimodal tasks by incorporating techniques like complementary masking for efficient training, prefix KV cache for fast inference (Prefix-DLM), and timestep shifting for better sample quality.  Experimental results show LaViDa achieving competitive or superior performance to AR VLMs on multimodal benchmarks like MMMU and demonstrating advantages in controllable generation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of building a VLM using diffusion models is a noteworthy contribution, as it explores a relatively underexplored direction compared to AR VLMs. The specific techniques used, complementary masking, Prefix-DLM, and timestep shifting, are individually innovative and well-motivated for addressing challenges in diffusion-based VLMs. However, the *combination* of these ideas and the demonstration within the VLM context constitutes the key innovation. The concept of bidirectional reasoning for VLMs has been touched in the past in AR context, but this gives it a natural place. The training scheme and image encoder is just from LLaVA.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from demonstrating the feasibility and potential advantages of using diffusion models for VLMs. This opens a new avenue for research in this domain, particularly for scenarios where controllability and flexible speed-quality tradeoffs are crucial. LaViDa provides a strong alternative to AR VLMs and offers potential benefits in areas like constrained generation and structured output formatting. The strong +4.1 Cider Score on COCO over the LLama3 models means that they solved the tokenization issues in prior DMs. Finally, the speedup is a huge boon for future work.
    However, some of these areas (such as controllable generation) are not fully explored quantitatively in the paper, which is a minor weakness. The OCR result is subpar, but the team acknowledges the issue with token compression.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating competitive performance on various multimodal benchmarks.
    *   Introduction of novel techniques that address specific challenges in adapting DMs for VLMs.
    *   Clear articulation of the advantages of using DMs for controllable generation and speed-quality tradeoffs.
    * The code release and model are released in public so the claims can be checked.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited exploration of the full potential of controllable generation through quantitative analysis of specific controlled generation tasks, while a qualitative exploration is done. More metrics could be shown for these areas.
    *   The method lags behind AR model OCR tasks.
    *   Although the components are novel, it does reuse the image encoder architecture and some components from the LLaVA model.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper can significantly influence the field by inspiring further research into diffusion-based VLMs. It could lead to the development of VLMs that are better suited for tasks requiring controllability, structured output, and flexible speed-quality tradeoffs.

*   **Justification:** While diffusion models in NLP have been established, their application to VLMs is less explored. The LaViDa paper makes a solid contribution by (a) demonstrating that diffusion models can achieve competitive performance with AR models in VLMs, and (b) providing specific techniques that make diffusion-based VLMs more effective (particularly regarding inference speed and controllability). The weaknesses are not major enough to detract from the overall novelty and significance of the work. In the future, this would get a higher score if these items are further developed.

**Score: 7.8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Walk&Retrieve: Simple Yet Effective Zero-shot Retrieval-Augmented Generation via Knowledge Graph Walks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16849v1)
- **Authors**: Martin Böckling, Heiko Paulheim, Andreea Iana
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have showcased impressive reasoning abilities, but often suffer from hallucinations or outdated knowledge. Knowledge Graph (KG)-based Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) remedies these shortcomings by grounding LLM responses in structured external information from a knowledge base. However, many KG-based RAG approaches struggle with (i) aligning KG and textual representations, (ii) balancing retrieval accuracy and efficiency, and (iii) adapting to dynamically updated KGs. In this work, we introduce Walk&Retrieve, a simple yet effective KG-based framework that leverages walk-based graph traversal and knowledge verbalization for corpus generation for zero-shot RAG. Built around efficient KG walks, our method does not require fine-tuning on domain-specific data, enabling seamless adaptation to KG updates, reducing computational overhead, and allowing integration with any off-the-shelf backbone LLM. Despite its simplicity, Walk&Retrieve performs competitively, often outperforming existing RAG systems in response accuracy and hallucination reduction. Moreover, it demonstrates lower query latency and robust scalability to large KGs, highlighting the potential of lightweight retrieval strategies as strong baselines for future RAG research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Walk&Retrieve, a simple yet effective zero-shot KG-based RAG framework. It addresses the limitations of existing KG-based RAG systems, such as aligning KG and textual representations, balancing retrieval accuracy and efficiency, and adapting to dynamically updated KGs. The core idea is to perform efficient graph traversals (random walks or breadth-first search) on a knowledge graph and then verbalize the resulting walks using an LLM. This creates a corpus of relevant facts for each entity in the KG. During inference, the system retrieves the most similar nodes and their corresponding walks to the input query, uses this context to prompt an LLM, and generates the final answer. A key advantage is that Walk&Retrieve doesn't require fine-tuning on domain-specific data, enabling adaptability to KG updates and integration with any off-the-shelf LLM. The paper demonstrates that Walk&Retrieve performs competitively, sometimes outperforming existing RAG systems, with lower query latency and better scalability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of the approach lies in the simplicity and effectiveness of combining KG walks with knowledge verbalization for zero-shot RAG. While the individual components (graph walks, knowledge verbalization, RAG) are not entirely new, the specific combination and the emphasis on zero-shot adaptability make this a notable contribution. The framework is quite straightforward, which is both a strength and a weakness. It's easy to implement and understand, but it might lack the sophistication of more complex approaches. The key differentiator is the system's adaptability to dynamic KGs without retraining. However, the novelty might be slightly incremental, building upon existing RAG and KG embedding techniques.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is that it presents a lightweight, scalable, and adaptable KG-based RAG framework that can be easily deployed and used with any off-the-shelf LLM. The experimental results demonstrate that Walk&Retrieve performs competitively, often outperforming more complex systems, while also minimizing hallucinations and reducing response times. This suggests that lightweight retrieval strategies can be strong baselines for future RAG research. The system also adapts better to dynamic KGs. This has practical implications for real-world applications where KGs are constantly evolving.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Simplicity and Efficiency:** The framework is easy to implement and understand, requiring only a single LLM call per query, which significantly reduces latency compared to methods that perform step-by-step reasoning.
    *   **Zero-Shot Adaptability:** The framework does not require fine-tuning on task-specific data, making it adaptable to new KGs and updates without the need for retraining.
    *   **Scalability:** The walk-based corpus generation limits traversal to small-hop neighborhoods, making it scalable to large KGs, and graph changes require only incremental updates to the corpus.
    *   **Hallucination Reduction:** The system minimizes hallucinations by grounding responses in the extracted structured knowledge and explicitly instructing the LLM to refrain from responding if the context is insufficient.
    *   The study compares the technique against multiple competitive baselines, including LLM only, vanilla RAG and knowledge graph RAG.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Complexity:** While simplicity is a strength, the framework may not be suitable for complex reasoning tasks that require more sophisticated graph traversal and reasoning techniques.
    *   **Corpus Quality:** The quality of the generated corpus depends on the chosen graph traversal strategy and the effectiveness of the knowledge verbalization process. Noisier contexts may affect the overall performance.
    *   **Limited Scalability Results:** While claimed to be scalable, the inability to run SubgraphRAG on CRAG raises questions about scalability.

    *   The description about which nodes and paths are retrieved in Section 2.2. isn't fully clear, and relies on the vague term "relevant", which would benefit from elaboration and justification.

    *   It is not immediately clear, if the graphs for the CRAG and MetaQA experiments are pre-existing, or derived from the benchmarks.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future RAG research by highlighting the effectiveness of lightweight retrieval strategies and promoting the development of more adaptable and scalable KG-based RAG systems.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of 7 out of 10.

*   The paper presents a clear and well-executed approach to zero-shot KG-based RAG with a focus on adaptability and scalability.

*   However, while effective, the individual components of the system are not entirely novel, and the approach relies on relatively simple graph traversal techniques. The claims of scalability in the face of CRAG are not adequately backed by experimental data. The discussion of section 2.2. can also be made more precise.

*   The paper's strengths lie in its simplicity, efficiency, and zero-shot adaptability, making it a practical solution for real-world applications where KGs are constantly evolving.

*   Overall, the paper is a solid contribution that can inspire future research, but is limited by the incremental improvement over prior art and only marginally new technical ideas.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Conditional Panoramic Image Generation via Masked Autoregressive Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16862v1)
- **Authors**: Chaoyang Wang, Xiangtai Li, Lu Qi, Xiaofan Lin, Jinbin Bai, Qianyu Zhou, Yunhai Tong
- **Abstract**: Recent progress in panoramic image generation has underscored two critical limitations in existing approaches. First, most methods are built upon diffusion models, which are inherently ill-suited for equirectangular projection (ERP) panoramas due to the violation of the identically and independently distributed (i.i.d.) Gaussian noise assumption caused by their spherical mapping. Second, these methods often treat text-conditioned generation (text-to-panorama) and image-conditioned generation (panorama outpainting) as separate tasks, relying on distinct architectures and task-specific data. In this work, we propose a unified framework, Panoramic AutoRegressive model (PAR), which leverages masked autoregressive modeling to address these challenges. PAR avoids the i.i.d. assumption constraint and integrates text and image conditioning into a cohesive architecture, enabling seamless generation across tasks. To address the inherent discontinuity in existing generative models, we introduce circular padding to enhance spatial coherence and propose a consistency alignment strategy to improve generation quality. Extensive experiments demonstrate competitive performance in text-to-image generation and panorama outpainting tasks while showcasing promising scalability and generalization capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Panoramic AutoRegressive Model (PAR), a novel framework for conditional panoramic image generation. Addressing limitations of existing diffusion-based methods, which struggle with equirectangular projection (ERP) panoramas due to the violation of the i.i.d. Gaussian noise assumption and task-specific pipelines, PAR leverages masked autoregressive modeling. The model unifies text-to-panorama, panorama outpainting, and image editing tasks within a single architecture. Key innovations include dual-space circular padding to enhance spatial coherence across ERP boundaries and a translation consistency loss to improve generation quality by minimizing discrepancies under shifted inputs. Experimental results demonstrate competitive performance in text-to-image generation and panorama outpainting compared to specialized models, along with promising scalability and generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers several contributions that enhance the field.
    *   The use of masked autoregressive modeling for panorama generation is a **significant shift** from the dominant diffusion-based approaches, circumventing inherent limitations related to the i.i.d. assumption when dealing with ERP projections. This is a **solid conceptual contribution**.
    *   The unification of text-to-panorama, outpainting, and editing into a single framework is valuable, reducing the need for task-specific architectures.
    *   The proposed dual-space circular padding and translation consistency loss are tailored to panoramic imagery. These address the unique challenges of ERP representations, such as boundary discontinuities and maintaining consistency across the 360-degree view.
*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is promising.
    *   By offering a unified and efficient framework, the PAR model can simplify the process of generating and manipulating panoramic images, potentially fostering advancements in VR/AR, autonomous driving, and related fields.
    *   The model addresses the i.i.d. assumption issues.
    *   The model's ability to perform various tasks, including generating the whole image and editing specific parts, will save a lot of time.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Unified Framework:** The paper presents a compelling case for a unified approach, which simplifies the pipeline for different panorama generation tasks.
    *   **Technically Sound:** The proposed circular padding and consistency loss are well-motivated and address specific challenges in panoramic image generation.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The paper provides quantitative results demonstrating competitive performance, especially on outpainting tasks, along with qualitative examples that highlight the model's advantages.
    *   **Scalability:** The experiments include results on varying model sizes, demonstrating the potential for scaling the model.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Quantitative Comparison:** The quantitative comparisons might be more extensive with more variety of evaluation metrics.
    *   **Lack of Details:** More detailed descriptions of the network architecture and training procedure might be included.
    *   **Runtime Evaluation:** The manuscript lacks a detailed evaluation of the runtime of the algorithm.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of panoramic image generation. The move away from diffusion-based models to autoregressive ones for panoramas is a smart approach, which addresses fundamental limitations. The dual-space circular padding and translation consistency loss are novel and tailored to the unique challenges of panoramic imagery. The unified framework is a practical advantage. While there is room for improvement, this work addresses a relevant problem with a creative solution and demonstrates tangible benefits.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Training-Free Efficient Video Generation via Dynamic Token Carving
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16864v1)
- **Authors**: Yuechen Zhang, Jinbo Xing, Bin Xia, Shaoteng Liu, Bohao Peng, Xin Tao, Pengfei Wan, Eric Lo, Jiaya Jia
- **Abstract**: Despite the remarkable generation quality of video Diffusion Transformer (DiT) models, their practical deployment is severely hindered by extensive computational requirements. This inefficiency stems from two key challenges: the quadratic complexity of self-attention with respect to token length and the multi-step nature of diffusion models. To address these limitations, we present Jenga, a novel inference pipeline that combines dynamic attention carving with progressive resolution generation. Our approach leverages two key insights: (1) early denoising steps do not require high-resolution latents, and (2) later steps do not require dense attention. Jenga introduces a block-wise attention mechanism that dynamically selects relevant token interactions using 3D space-filling curves, alongside a progressive resolution strategy that gradually increases latent resolution during generation. Experimental results demonstrate that Jenga achieves substantial speedups across multiple state-of-the-art video diffusion models while maintaining comparable generation quality (8.83$\times$ speedup with 0.01\% performance drop on VBench). As a plug-and-play solution, Jenga enables practical, high-quality video generation on modern hardware by reducing inference time from minutes to seconds -- without requiring model retraining. Code: https://github.com/dvlab-research/Jenga
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Training-Free Efficient Video Generation via Dynamic Token Carving" paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces Jenga, a training-free inference pipeline designed to accelerate video generation using Diffusion Transformer (DiT) models. Jenga addresses the computational bottlenecks of DiT models by:

1.  **Dynamic Attention Carving:** Minimizing token interactions through a dynamic, sparse attention mechanism that selects relevant token interactions using 3D space-filling curves and block-wise attention. This selectively computes key-value pairs, achieving sparse attention.

2.  **Progressive Resolution (ProRes) Generation:** Generating video through phased resizing and denoising of latents, reducing token interactions. A text-attention amplifier is used to maintain field of view when using low-resolution initial stages.

Jenga demonstrates significant speedups on several state-of-the-art video diffusion models while maintaining comparable generation quality. The paper showcases results on HunyuanVideo, AccVideo, and Wan2.1, reducing inference times from minutes to seconds without requiring model retraining.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of dynamic attention carving and progressive resolution generation is novel. Prior works have focused on either operator-based acceleration or pipeline optimization (e.g., distillation, quantization), but Jenga proposes a combination of two techniques that are demonstrated to be complementary. The attention carving with space-filling curves addresses the issue of massive token length in self-attention models specifically. The progress resolution strategy coupled with the text-attention amplifier has not been investigated before.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of this work lies in its ability to drastically reduce the inference time of video diffusion models without retraining. This addresses a critical barrier to the practical deployment of these models. The authors demonstrate significant speedups, reducing inference time from minutes to seconds on commodity hardware. This opens the door for real-time or near-real-time video generation applications. The fact that Jenga is a plug-and-play solution further enhances its practical value.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The techniques are generally applicable and demonstrated across different DiT-based architectures.
    *   Significant speedups are achieved while maintaining generation quality.
    *   Jenga is a training-free approach, saving considerable resources.
    *   The integration with multi-GPU parallel processing demonstrates scalability.
    *   User studies suggest the results are perceptually comparable to other efficient generation approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The results have been shown to produce boundary artifcats in the presence of some images or more complex texture.
    *   The paper lacks a theoretical analysis of the attention carving performance, such as whether the performance is guaranteed under some assumptions on the underlying latent space.
    *   The current approach uses a non-adaptive static SFC block partitioning.
    *   The quality metrics for assessing video generation can be improved by incorporating more recent evaluation measures.

*   **Impact:** This paper presents a significant step toward making video diffusion models more practical. The proposed techniques can accelerate video generation and make it more accessible for real-world applications. It also provides a clear direction for future research.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a highly practical and valuable approach to accelerating video diffusion models. The techniques are novel and demonstrably effective across multiple architectures. While some limitations remain, this work represents a significant contribution to the field of efficient video generation, warranting a strong score. The rigorous evaluation, integration into existing architectures, and demonstration of substantial speedups highlight its value. It is only limited by potential artifacts introduced, lack of a theoretical analysis, the use of static SF block partioning, and use of outdated quality metrics. The score is therefore within the 8-9 bracket.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### MPO: Multilingual Safety Alignment via Reward Gap Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16869v1)
- **Authors**: Weixiang Zhao, Yulin Hu, Yang Deng, Tongtong Wu, Wenxuan Zhang, Jiahe Guo, An Zhang, Yanyan Zhao, Bing Qin, Tat-Seng Chua, Ting Liu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have become increasingly central to AI applications worldwide, necessitating robust multilingual safety alignment to ensure secure deployment across diverse linguistic contexts. Existing preference learning methods for safety alignment, such as RLHF and DPO, are primarily monolingual and struggle with noisy multilingual data. To address these limitations, we introduce Multilingual reward gaP Optimization (MPO), a novel approach that leverages the well-aligned safety capabilities of the dominant language (English) to improve safety alignment across multiple languages. MPO directly minimizes the reward gap difference between the dominant language and target languages, effectively transferring safety capabilities while preserving the original strengths of the dominant language. Extensive experiments on three LLMs, LLaMA-3.1, Gemma-2 and Qwen2.5, validate MPO's efficacy in multilingual safety alignment without degrading general multilingual utility.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "MPO: Multilingual Safety Alignment via Reward Gap Optimization," a novel approach to improve the safety alignment of large language models (LLMs) across multiple languages.  MPO leverages the robust safety alignment of a dominant language (e.g., English) as a high-quality supervision signal to align other languages. It achieves this by directly minimizing the reward gap discrepancy between the dominant and target languages, preserving the dominant language's strengths while improving safety in other languages. The authors demonstrate MPO's effectiveness on LLaMA-3.1, Gemma-2, and Qwen2.5 models across various multilingual safety benchmarks, showing improved safety without compromising general utility. The paper emphasizes the use of a well-aligned reward gap as a crucial indicator of safety performance and a reliable supervision signal for multilingual alignment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the use of reward gap *discrepancy minimization* as a means of transferring safety alignment from a dominant language to other languages. While preference learning methods like DPO and RLHF are established, directly minimizing the *difference* in reward gaps to transfer capabilities is a distinct contribution. The analysis highlighting the correlation between the reward gap and multilingual safety performance provides empirical backing for this approach. The method also incorporates a representation constraint to preserve the original capability of the dominant language, another unique aspect.
*   **Significance:** The work addresses a crucial problem in the deployment of LLMs: ensuring safety across diverse linguistic contexts. Many existing safety alignment methods are primarily monolingual or struggle with noisy multilingual data. MPO offers a practical solution for transferring safety capabilities, potentially reducing the need for extensive and high-quality multilingual training data for every language. This is particularly relevant for low-resource languages, where data scarcity is a significant challenge. The experimental results, demonstrating improved safety across multiple models and benchmarks, indicate the practical relevance of the method.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Empirical Foundation:** The empirical analysis linking reward gap and safety performance is compelling.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of multilingual safety alignment and the limitations of existing methods.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation covers multiple LLMs, a variety of multilingual safety benchmarks, and addresses general utility and robustness to data noise and source.
    *   **Ablation Studies:**  The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components of MPO.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Google Translate API:** The dependence on Google Translate API for generating multilingual training data may introduce biases and impact the quality of the training data, especially for low-resource languages. While the paper addresses data quality to some extent, a more thorough investigation of the effects of translation quality could be beneficial.
    *   **Limited investigation of languages beyond the set used for the experiment**: While the paper looks at varying data qualities for the same set of languages, it would be useful to know how the MPO method would perform with languages that were more different than the set used for the experiment (for instance, including right-to-left or top-to-bottom languages).
    *   **Lack of comparison with certain cross-lingual alignment methods**: The comparison includes certain cross-lingual transfer methodes but omits a comparison to instruction alignment methods like those used for training multilingual LLMs.

*   **Potential Influence:** The work has the potential to influence future research on multilingual LLM alignment. The concept of reward gap discrepancy minimization could be adopted and extended by others. The empirical findings and analyses provide valuable insights for the development of more robust and scalable multilingual safety alignment techniques. The analysis of reward gap provides a simple approach for understanding the safety alignment of LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by proposing a novel and practical approach to multilingual safety alignment. The core idea of minimizing the reward gap discrepancy shows potential for efficient transfer of safety capabilities. The clear problem definition, solid empirical foundation, comprehensive evaluation, and insights from ablation studies are strengths. While the reliance on Google Translate API and limited scope in language set and comparison represents limitations, the paper addresses a critical challenge and provides a novel direction for future research. A score of 7 reflects the paper's sound methodology, promising results, and potential to influence future work in the field, while also accounting for some of its limitations.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### T2I-ConBench: Text-to-Image Benchmark for Continual Post-training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16875v1)
- **Authors**: Zhehao Huang, Yuhang Liu, Yixin Lou, Zhengbao He, Mingzhen He, Wenxing Zhou, Tao Li, Kehan Li, Zeyi Huang, Xiaolin Huang
- **Abstract**: Continual post-training adapts a single text-to-image diffusion model to learn new tasks without incurring the cost of separate models, but naive post-training causes forgetting of pretrained knowledge and undermines zero-shot compositionality. We observe that the absence of a standardized evaluation protocol hampers related research for continual post-training. To address this, we introduce T2I-ConBench, a unified benchmark for continual post-training of text-to-image models. T2I-ConBench focuses on two practical scenarios, item customization and domain enhancement, and analyzes four dimensions: (1) retention of generality, (2) target-task performance, (3) catastrophic forgetting, and (4) cross-task generalization. It combines automated metrics, human-preference modeling, and vision-language QA for comprehensive assessment. We benchmark ten representative methods across three realistic task sequences and find that no approach excels on all fronts. Even joint "oracle" training does not succeed for every task, and cross-task generalization remains unsolved. We release all datasets, code, and evaluation tools to accelerate research in continual post-training for text-to-image models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "T2I-ConBench: Text-to-Image Benchmark for Continual Post-training":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces T2I-ConBench, a unified benchmark for continual post-training of text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models. The benchmark focuses on two practical scenarios: item customization and domain enhancement.  It evaluates performance across four dimensions: retention of generality, target-task performance, catastrophic forgetting, and cross-task generalization, using a combination of automated metrics, human preference modeling, and vision-language question answering.
